# de: per-band report

Bands are positions in the cleaned list (the kept count), so a band holds what a cut there would keep. Merged lemmas leave the ranking (their counts join the target) and are counted where they stood. This report is evidence for the developer's cutoff decision; it does not make it.

| band | entries | keep | review-keep | review-drop | merged | kept share | real among reviewed |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 0-10k | 11,043 | 6,765 | 3,235 | 1,043 | 250 | 90.6% | 77.0% of 4,528 |
| 10-20k | 12,279 | 8,153 | 1,847 | 2,279 | 828 | 81.4% | 54.0% of 4,954 |
| 20-25k | 6,388 | 4,144 | 856 | 1,388 | 535 | 78.3% | 50.1% of 2,779 |
| 25-30k | 6,718 | 4,059 | 941 | 1,718 | 671 | 74.4% | 48.4% of 3,330 |
| 30-35k | 6,829 | 4,372 | 628 | 1,829 | 904 | 73.2% | 45.6% of 3,361 |
| 35-40k | 6,934 | 4,434 | 566 | 1,934 | 955 | 72.1% | 44.0% of 3,455 |

## Questions for the developer (1635 lemmas; first question per lemma)

# question Abbrucharbeit: written almost always as a plural; kept as the singular lemma.
# question CERN: counted as a research institute (drop), but it is also nationally well known
# question Clogs: kept as a plural-only everyday form rather than merging into Clog
# question Entrepreneur: English/French loan, rare in German; dropped as not established enough
# question Havana: the German spelling is Havanna; here the lemma is mostly a club name, so dropped
# question Hoover: could be a genericised brand, but the examples show the surname
# question Kleinasien: a major historical region that people might expect on the learnable places list, but the rules say any place name still in the batch is dropped
# question Menstruationsbeschwerde: the citation flag says only the plural is written, but the lemma is already the singular dictionary form; I used merge to itself, which should probably be treated as keep.
# question Schleswig-Holsteinische: unclear whether the state's adjective counts as a listed place adjective; merged to the base adjective form.
# question Webfonts: unclear whether the specialist term Webfont is learnable; merged on the plural rule.
# question Wehwehchen: the flagged lemma is spelled Wehwehche, but the list entry given is "Wehwehchen" in the heading as shown; I decided on the real word Wehwehchen, so merge:Wehwehchen may be the intended action.
# question mess: counts are mostly Messer and Messe; decided by the lemma's own English reading
# question Wester: the counts are mostly "western" (genre loan vs English text or names like Western Union); should the lemmatiser split these before Western is judged?
# question Apex: the counts are mostly company names, but I decided by the word's own reading (rule 13).
# question Geschriebene: "das Geschriebene" could count as a lexicalised nominalisation like Angestellte, but the counts are lower-case inflected adjective forms, so I merged it into schreiben.
# question Leggin: the counts are almost all "leggins", so merging to Leggings assumes that is the intended headword.
# question Madrider: I assumed Madrid is on the learnable place list; drop if it is not.
# question Stealth: it could count as an established loan (Stealth-Technologie), but the counts are mostly product names, so I dropped it.
# question Washingtoner: I assumed Washington is on the learnable place list; drop if it is not.
# question gung: no obvious host word for this fragment, so I dropped it instead of merging.
# question kit: kiten (61 counts) suggests a kitesurfing verb, but I merged it into the noun Kit.
# question rhodiniert: technical participle; merged to rhodinieren on the participle rule, though it could also be dropped as specialist vocabulary.
# question vitro: "in vitro" is a fixed Latin phrase; dropped as a foreign fragment, but could be argued as a learnable phrase.
# question zurich: Zürich may be on the learnable list, which would make this a variant spelling to merge; I dropped it because the rules say any place name still shown is not on the list.
# question Madrider, Washingtoner, zurich: please confirm that Madrid, Washington and Zürich are on the learnable place list; the rule 1 keeps and the rule 2 merge depend on it
# question Metropolis: counts are mostly the film and cinema names, but the word itself is a real German noun; kept under ruling 13.
# question Mossad: foreign institution; kept as nationally famous and often mentioned in German news.
# question VVK: abbreviation of Vorverkauf; ruling 12 would keep it like Lkw/Kita, but the general abbreviation rule says drop. I dropped it.
# question Yankee: counts are mostly the Yankee Candle brand; decided by the word's own reading (ruling 13).
# question Istanbuler: unclear whether Istanbul is on the learnable place list; kept on the assumption it is (drop if not)
# question Theresienwiese: borderline between landmark (keep) and ordinary place name (drop); kept as a famous site
# question bye: "bye" is a common informal interjection in German and could count as an established loan; I dropped it as an English word.
# question Fatigue: medical term used in German texts on cancer, but not established in general German; dropped as an English word.
# question Locher: tagged PROPN, but the examples read mostly as the common noun (hole punch), so kept; some occurrences are surnames.
# question Capo: could also be the mafia/foreman loan "Capo", but the examples show only the place-name use, so I dropped it.
# question Sprachenlerne: Merge target is the nominalisation Sprachenlernen, which is lexicalised in compounds and so kept as its own entry rather than merged to the verb. The lemma may not exist on the list, so check it is there.
# question VKA: Vereinigung der kommunalen Arbeitgeberverbände is a minor organisation, but it could count as nationally known in public-sector pay. I treated it as a minor organisation.
# question AFD: written in capitals AFD; kept as the party acronym, spelling variant of AfD
# question Anfahr: target could be the verb anfahren or the nominalised Anfahren; chose the noun as the examples use it capitalised
# question AntifaschistInnen: the gendered plural form could also be filed under Antifaschistin; merged to Antifaschist.
# question Ardenn: merged to Ardennen, but Ardennen is a region that may not be on the learnable places list
# question Aufsetz: verb aufsetzen or nominalised Aufsetzen? Merged to the verb since the forms are all infinitive-based.
# question BFD: could be Bundesfreiwilligendienst (a nationally known institution, which would be keep), but the example reads as an organisation-specific application template, so dropped
# question BMZ: a federal ministry acronym could count as a nationally famous institution, but it is little known by name and the examples include a non-ministry use; I dropped it.
# question BNP: it is both an ordinary abbreviation (Bruttonationalprodukt) and the bank BNP Paribas; dropped because the examples are mostly the bank
# question Bosnien-Herzegowina: a country that should have been taken out already; dropped per the "any place name you still see" rule
# question Cayenne: ambiguous between the spice (common noun) and the Porsche model; kept for the spice sense.
# question Chai: loan word for the tea, but not clearly established enough in German; kept
# question Clouds: I merged it into Cloud as a plural, but the English-plural spelling may be debatable.
# question Coworking: tagged PROPN but used as a common loan; kept as established.
# question Cyberpunk: genre loan word, but the evidence is almost all the game title, so I dropped it.
# question DECT: it could count as a technical acronym, but it is a consumer term in Germany, so I kept it.
# question DIW: counted it as nationally famous like KfW, but it is borderline versus a minor organisation
# question DRV: Deutsche Rentenversicherung might count as a nationally famous institution, but examples mix in a racing association
# question DVR: Deutscher Verkehrssicherheitsrat is a minor body; dropped, though it could count as nationally known
# question Din: lowercase "din" may be the DIN norm or a name, with no usable examples; I dropped it.
# question EBA: Eisenbahn-Bundesamt might count as a national body, but it is little known to learners; dropped
# question EGMR: a court acronym; I treated it as a nationally known institution and kept it, but it could count as a specialist acronym.
# question EMRK: a famous treaty acronym, but not listed as nationally famous institution; dropped as a specialist acronym
# question Eckdat: Eckdaten is effectively plural-only, so I merged to the plural form rather than a singular
# question Eislaufen: the lemma is mostly a nominalised infinitive (das Eislaufen), which could count as a noun in its own right; I merged it into the verb.
# question ExpertInn: the target could be Experte or Expertin; chose Experte as the generic masculine lemma.
# question Extensions: hair extensions are a loan used in German, but the plural filed as a lemma was dropped as English
# question FRAGEN: could also be the plural of Frage, but the lower-case spelling points to the verb fragen
# question GLP: Swiss party acronym; rules name CDU/SPD as keep but GLP is a minor foreign party, dropped
# question Gefickt: vulgar and concentrated in porn spam, so merging it to ficken may not suit a learner list; the rules say to merge participle forms
# question Geodat: Geodaten is effectively plural-only, so I merged into the plural form rather than a singular.
# question Goliath: also used as a common noun for a giant opponent ("David gegen Goliath"), but with 0 % name-like evidence I dropped it as a biblical name.
# question HPV: it could count as a technical acronym only specialists use, but laypeople know it from vaccination talk, so I kept it.
# question Hausmann: tagged as a name, but plural and genitive forms show common-noun use; kept.
# question Helmholtz-Zentrum: may count as a nationally famous institution (Helmholtz-Gemeinschaft), but it is a generic label for many individual centres, so I dropped it.
# question Hofburg: it is a Vienna place name, but also a common noun for a royal palace; I kept it as a common noun.
# question Hopp: could be the interjection *hopp*, but the inflected forms hopps/hoppen point to a surname; I dropped it.
# question IPO: finance acronym; unclear whether it counts as an ordinary word or a specialist acronym
# question IT-Services: the rules don't say whether an English plural in a hyphenated loan compound should be merged to the singular, so I merged it.
# question Jahwe: religious name, not covered by the rules for people or institutions. I dropped it.
# question KGaA: legal-form acronym treated like GmbH, but it could count as a company-style acronym.
# question KPCh: acronym of the Chinese Communist Party; unclear whether foreign party acronyms count as nationally famous, dropped.
# question Kali: could be mostly Kali Linux or the goddess; kept as the potash noun
# question Kfz-Steuer#Neut: merged to the clean hyphenated compound; the Kfz abbreviation rule applies only to the bare abbreviation.
# question Kiewer: Kiew is a place name that may be on the reviewed list; if not, drop would apply.
# question KundInnen: could merge to Kunde or Kundin; chose Kunde as the generic form
# question Kürbiskerne: the plural is the usual form, but I merged it to the singular as the rule says.
# question LOL: internet slang acronym; kept as an ordinary word, but it could also count as informal chat-only text
# question Lesbos: well-known Greek island, but not on the developer-added list and no reviewed-list evidence; dropped per the rule
# question Liane: a real noun (liana) but the examples are mostly the first name; I kept it.
# question Lid: tagger and wordfreq suggest English, but the inflected forms (lides, liden) look like German Lid (eyelid)
# question Logs: the English plural may not be the German Log; merged on the assumption that Log is established in German IT usage
# question Ludwig-Maximilians-Universität: a university name is not listed among the famous institutions; kept as nationally well known
# question MMA: could be read as a sports-organisation acronym, but it is used as an ordinary word for the sport, so I kept it.
# question Melk: mostly the Austrian town, but "melken" (50 occurrences) is a real verb filed under it; dropped as a place name rather than merged.
# question Memoir: filed lemma is the singular, but the text is almost all memoiren; merged to the plural-only noun Memoiren
# question MfS: an acronym for a historical institution, not on the explicit institution list. I kept it as nationally known.
# question Moldawien: rules say remaining place names drop, but Moldawien is a country; I dropped it per the rule, though it may belong on the reviewed list.
# question Moll: the only sampled example is a surname, but Moll is also the common music term (a-Moll); kept for the music sense.
# question Nordhesse: lemma means a person from North Hesse, but nearly all uses are the region name Nordhessen; I dropped it as a minor region.
# question Nordstadt: district name in the examples but also a common noun; kept as common noun
# question Nude: colour word in fashion, tagged PROPN; kept as an established loan but borderline
# question Nutzungsdat: same as Eckdat, Nutzungsdaten is used almost only in the plural
# question OLED: consumer-known display term or specialist technical acronym? I kept it.
# question Offshore: it is an English loan used mostly in compounds; I judged it established enough to keep
# question PCR: a technical acronym, kept only because it became everyday vocabulary
# question Pancakes: it is mostly used in the plural, so merging to the singular Pancake follows the plural rule but may not suit.
# question Phi: could count as a common noun (Greek letter), but there are no examples, so I dropped it.
# question Polizeikraft: kept as the plural-only-type noun Polizeikräfte, but the singular lemma form is rarely written as a word
# question RECHT: lemma is all-caps and written "recht" (441). It could be the adjective/adverb *recht* rather than the noun *Recht*; I merged to the noun.
# question Reck: kept as the gymnastics noun, but the tag and the missing examples suggest it may mostly be a surname
# question Registration: it is a real German noun, mostly Swiss and technical use, while Registrierung is the usual word. I kept it.
# question Reykjavik: a well-known capital, but the rules say any place name still in the batch is not on the learnable list, so I dropped it.
# question Ries: 19.5% common-noun-like could be the paper measure or the Nördlinger Ries region, but it is also a surname; kept on that evidence
# question SSL-Zertifikat: SSL alone drops as a technical acronym, but the compound is common in everyday web text; kept
# question Scala: could be the Milan opera house (name) or a miswritten Skala; dropped as a name.
# question Schiffer: also a rare common noun (boatman), but the evidence reads as a surname; dropped
# question Seenland: regional name (Fränkisches Seenland) but also a real common compound (31.5% after adjective); dropped as a place name not on the list
# question Selbstbewusstseinstraining: concentrated in 26 documents with brand-like text (IntSel); kept because it is a real compound.
# question Slide: "Slides" is used in business German, but the evidence here is only a game name, so I dropped it.
# question Spotlight: the phrase "im Spotlight" is used in German, but the sample is mostly software and product use. I dropped it.
# question Stumpf: capitalised PROPN with mixed usage; kept as the noun Stumpf and adjective stumpf, may need a case merge to stumpf for the adjective forms
# question TUN: the lemma is all-caps and tagged PROPN, but every occurrence is written "tun", so I read it as the verb.
# question Tabellenzweit: the noun is inflected like an adjective (Tabellenzweiter); I chose the masculine form
# question Tages-Anzeiger: a Swiss newspaper may count as a nationally famous institution, but I treated it as a title and dropped it.
# question Tagungs-Bahn: could be read as a clipped Tagung-compound fragment, but there is no obvious single target, so dropped
# question Tasting: kept as an established loan, but it is mainly used in wine and spirits contexts.
# question Thanksgiving: a foreign-flagged holiday name; kept as a common noun, but it could be read as a name or an unestablished English word.
# question VWL: abbreviation by shape rules (would drop), but acronym used as an ordinary word in payroll/economics contexts; kept as an acronym word
# question Visit: 75 of the 403 occurrences are "visiten" (Visite, medical round); mostly English "visit", so dropped
# question Web-Sit: the rules list Website as an established loan, so I merged into it; the spelling variant Web-Site could also be the target.
# question Workspace: could count as an established tech loan; dropped as not yet established.
# question Zeitgrund: only the plural is attested, in the set phrase "aus Zeitgründen", so I kept it as the singular lemma.
# question Zip: tagger reads it as a name, but it may count as a loan for the file format; dropped as not established.
# question aufsetzen: the noun-tagged lemma Aufsetz could also merge to the nominalised Aufsetzen; I chose the verb.
# question auserkiesen: the lemma is the dictionary form but only participle forms occur; kept as a rare real verb.
# question diplomieren: the text is almost all participles (diplomierte); kept as the verb lemma, but diplomiert could also be argued.
# question eu: lemma mixes the acronym EU (426) with euer inflections (euen, eue, euem, eues); I kept it as EU
# question lebensretten: the forms are all the adjective lebensrettend, which I chose as the target; check that lebensrettend is a wanted lemma
# question mind: lemma mixes mind/minden/mindes with no example sentences; dropped as junk, but it might be an abbreviation of mindestens
# question namenhaft: the correct spelling is namhaft; the lemma is a common misspelling of it
# question nicht-kommerziell: kept as hyphenated form, though the standard spelling is nichtkommerziell
# question release: "Release" is used in German IT contexts, but this lemma is lower-case and tagged ADJ, so I dropped it rather than merging to Release.
# question sechsmonatig: the citation flag is triggered by inflected-only spelling, but the dictionary form is a real adjective; kept as is.
# question ÖSTERREICH: Österreich is a country that should have been taken out of the batch; merged into the normal spelling.
# question Überling: the lemma is a clipped form of Überlingen; I dropped it because the town is not on the learnable list, so the merge target would also be dropped.
# question Bosnien-Herzegowina, Moldawien: both are sovereign countries left in the batch; should they be added to the learnable place list, as Mazedonien and Weißrussland were?
# question GLP, DIW: does "nationally famous institution" cover Swiss and Austrian national parties, and research institutes that are often cited in the news?
# question KundInnen, MitarbeiterInnen, AktivistInnen, AntifaschistInn, AutorInn: Binnen-I / gender plurals mean both sexes. I merged them all to the feminine singular, the form they inflect as. Should they go to the masculine base (Kunde, Mitarbeiter) instead?

# question ju: "ju" (289) and "juden" (45) are mixed, so I dropped it on the majority form
# question match: English "Match" is used in German sports contexts, but there are no examples and the lemma is lower-case with mixed noun and verb forms; I dropped it, so keep or merge:Match could also fit.
# question nachreden: lemma also covers the verb nachreden (nachgeredet, nachzureden), but nearly all uses are the noun Nachrede, so merged to the noun
# question nahend: participle form of nahen, but it may also stand as its own adjective; decided merge:nahen
# question nächsthoch: the dictionary form is unclear (nächsthöher as comparative, or nächsthoch); I merged to nächsthöher, the most frequent bare form
# question nürnberg: a well-known city, but it was not taken out of the batch, so I dropped it
# question review: English but common in German web text; kept as an established loan
# question rigid: merged into the dictionary form rigide despite the foreign flag, since it is a real German adjective.
# question timelin: Timeline is an established loan in social-media German but may still count as English.
# question untergehend: participle with its own adjective use in the phrase "untergehende Sonne"; kept as dictionary-form adjective though the verb untergehen is the alternative

# question pending-names: the answer format allows this decision, but the rules do not define it; when should it be used instead of drop?
# question AG: Aktiengesellschaft may not be on the list, so I dropped it as an abbreviation instead of merging
# question AGBs: AGB is plural-only and may not be on the list as its own lemma; I chose merge:AGB.
# question Aal: counts are dominated by the city Aalen, but the lemma Aal is a valid noun; I kept it.
# question Affiliate: borderline between marketing jargon and an established loan. I dropped it.
# question Anm: I assumed Anmerkung is on the list; if it is not, the abbreviation should be dropped.
# question Arcade: The examples are mostly English names, but the plural arcaden suggests the architectural sense, so I merged it into Arkade.
# question Au: Interjection "au" is a real word but the data shows no examples; dropped.

# question BH: Lkw and BH are abbreviations but ordinary spoken words; kept rather than merged or dropped, though the screener compared Lkw to excluded Kfz
# question BMI: very common in everyday German, but I treated it as a specialist acronym and dropped it.
# question BVerfG: abbreviation of a famous institution (Bundesverfassungsgericht); keep as a famous institution or merge/drop as an abbreviation?
# question Bamberger: it is a demonym and the adjective of a place name, and the rules don't cover demonyms; dropped
# question Band: the attested form "bandes" fits der Band, das Band and die Band, so the gender is unclear; kept as a valid noun lemma.
# question Basics: it is a plural loan with no common singular; I kept it as an established loan.
# question Bemühe: written forms are noun forms (Bemühen, Bemühens), so I merged to the noun Bemühen rather than the verb bemühen.
# question Besondere: the dictionary adjective headword is "besonder" or "besondere", and I'm not sure which one is on the list.
# question Bj: Baujahr may be on the list, in which case this should be merge:Baujahr
# question Blogg: surface forms are mostly the verb/nominalised bloggen, but the noun Blog also occurs; chose bloggen as the dominant form
# question Blowjob: the rules don't say whether vulgar loans stay in a learner list
# question Bonner: same as Mainzer, a place-derived adjective, dropped.
# question Bordeaux: also a wine and a dog breed (Bordeauxdogge), but the examples are mostly the place name, so dropped
# question Borke: lemma is a valid noun but the counts come from the town Borken, so I kept it on the strength of the word itself
# question Brandenburger: place-derived adjective/inhabitant noun is not a place name itself; dropped as a name derived from an unlisted place.
# question Braunschweiger: demonym adjectives from place names (Braunschweiger, Allgäuer, Osnabrücker) are not covered explicitly; I treated them like place names and dropped them.
# question Brüsseler: A place-derived adjective could merge into the place name, but Brüssel is not on the list, so I dropped it. Vorarlberger is handled the same way.
# question Buddha: a religious figure's name, but also a common noun (a Buddha statue); kept on that basis, though "other names drop" could apply
# question CA: the example is California (CA), but the forms are mostly "ca." = circa, and I assumed circa is on the list.
# question CL: units cl and ml (and wk, HG) are unclear in the abbreviation rule; dropped as abbreviations not clearly tied to a listed word.
# question Char: "char/chars" looks like English or programming usage, but a rare German "Char" (fish) is possible; I dropped it.
# question Charts: could be merged into Chart, but it is effectively plural-only in use. I kept it.
# question Cop: slang loan; borderline between established loan and English word, so I kept it
# question Dino: may be a clipped form of Dinosaurier (merge) or a name; kept as an established colloquial word
# question Disc: could be a variant of Disk or an English loan, and no example sentence was available
# question Diversity: English word widely used in German corporate texts; unclear whether it counts as an established loan.
# question Drum: could be the colloquial "drum" (darum) or the English "drum(s)"; neither is clearly learnable, so dropped
# question Dunkle: forms dunkeln/dunklen come from "im Dunkeln" (noun Dunkel), though an adjective dunkel is also possible.
# question EC: could be a specialist acronym or the common EC-Karte; dropped as an abbreviation
# question EM: merged into Europameisterschaft on the assumption that it is on the list; if not, EM may be better kept as a common abbreviation.
# question Edeka: Rules say famous institutions keep, but not whether major companies count. I kept Edeka and Swisscom as such.
# question Emde: it is a fragment of Emden, but Emden is a place name not on the list, so merging would only point to a dropped lemma; I dropped it.
# question EnBW: companies are not covered by the "famous institutions" rule; dropped EnBW, Medion and Playtech as other names.
# question Ergo: the screener flagged it as a company name, but the forms fit the Latin loan adverb; I chose keep.
# question Eurojackpot: a specific lottery name, so it could count as a product name; I kept it as a common noun.
# question FH: could merge into Fachhochschule if that is listed; dropped as an abbreviation
# question FR: the examples were not available, so merging into für rests on the form "fr" with the umlaut lost, as in whrend.
# question Fallout: mostly the game title in examples, but the noun is also in German use; kept
# question Fed: it is a short form of a famous institution, but the rules also drop abbreviations; I kept it.
# question Finn: could also be read as a first name (drop); I kept it as the nationality noun.
# question Flip: Duden lists Flip (a drink, a jump), but the examples are product or name uses; dropped.
# question Folgende: unclear whether "folgend" exists as a lemma, or whether the participial adjective should fold into the verb folgen
# question Folk: the screeners doubted it, but Folk is in Duden as a loan; kept.
# question Franken: the examples show the currency, so I kept it; the region sense would be a place name and a drop
# question Freu: the lemma for freuen could be "sich freuen" (pronominal) rather than "freuen"; merged to the plain verb
# question Friede: dictionary form is also Frieden, but Friede is a legitimate lemma; the surface form frieder is mostly the first name Frieder
# question Fu: the written forms fu/fuen suggest a lost ß (Fuß/Füßen), but it could also be a fragment; I merged it to Fuß.
# question Füsse: counted forms are mostly "füssen", which may be the town Füssen; merged to Fuß as the plural/Swiss spelling.
# question Geschäftsleute: could be a plural of Geschäftsmann/-frau (merge) or a plural-only noun like Leute (keep); I kept it.
# question Glut: written forms are mostly Gluten (the protein, a noun in its own right), but the lemma Glut is also a real word; I kept it.
# question Gr: could be a cut-off of "groß" or "Größe", or an abbreviation; no clear target, so dropped
# question Grazer: a demonym or adjective derived from a place name; the rules cover only place names, so I treated it as a drop
# question Grundkenntnis: it occurs almost only in the plural; I kept it as a plural-only noun, but the singular form is in the lemma.
# question Gute: the nominalised use ("Alles Gute") might justify keeping it as a noun, but most written forms are lower-case adjectives
# question Hamburger: it could also be the food noun, but all the examples are the demonym
# question Heidelberger: rules do not say whether demonyms (Heidelberger, Aachener, Bochumer, Mannheimer) count as place names; I dropped all four.
# question Helles: the lemma is really "heller" in the text, and the examples are the surname Heller; unclear whether to merge to hell, so I dropped it.
# question Hl: could be Hektoliter or heilig, so I dropped it rather than guess a merge target
# question Hoster: borderline loan; dropped as not established.
# question Huawei: well-known brand; treated as an "other name" and dropped
# question Hunderttausende: spelled-out number compounds have no explicit rule, so I kept it as a nominalised plural
# question Hyundai: it is a brand, and the rules do not say whether a brand counts as a famous institution; I dropped it, as I did Lenovo.
# question Hz: the rules cover abbreviations of listed words, but it is also a specialist unit. Merged to Hertz.
# question Höchste: merged to hoch, but höchst could also count as its own entry.
# question IM: it is unclear whether im is itself a lemma or should be merged into in; I merged to im.
# question IP: it is widely used (IP-Adresse), so it may count as an established loan; I dropped it as a specialist acronym.
# question Jakobsweg: it could count as a route name, but it is a common noun with plural forms; kept
# question Joche: Merging into Joch would be wrong given the Jochen name evidence; dropped.
# question Jupiter: "other names drop" may cover planet names, but I treated it as a dictionary word and kept it
# question Juso: Short form of Jungsozialist and a party-youth-wing term; unclear whether it counts as an abbreviation or an organisation name. I kept it as an established common noun.
# question Jüngere: kept Mächtige as a noun but merged Jüngere into jung, because it is a comparative; the rules do not say how to treat nominalised adjectives
# question Jüngste: it is an inflected superlative, but as a nominalised person noun (die Jüngste) I treated it as a noun in its own right; merge:jung is the alternative.
# question KW: could be Kalenderwoche or Kilowatt, no clear single listed word; dropped as an abbreviation.
# question KZ: abbreviation of a word that may not be on the list; kept as a well-known German term
# question Karlsruher: place-name adjectives are not covered explicitly; dropped on the assumption that the place-name rule extends to them.
# question Kasseler: it is also a noun for the pork dish and for residents, but the data shows mostly the place adjective; I dropped it as place-derived.
# question Kat: it could be Katalysator or Katze, and the forms kates and kats fit either; I chose Katalysator.
# question Kehl: the forms mix the city Kehl with the noun Kehle (602 uses). I dropped it as a place name, but Kehle should be checked against the list.
# question Kg: assumes Kilogramm is on the list; if it is not, this should be drop
# question Kleinste: nominalised "die Kleinsten" (small children) could count as a noun in its own right; I treated it as an inflected superlative of klein.
# question Knowhow: unclear whether it should merge into the standard spelling Know-how if that is listed.
# question Kollage: the screeners said misspelling of Collage, but the forms are kollagen (collagen), so I merged into Kollagen.
# question Kreml: it could count as a famous institution (keep), but I treated it as a place name not on the list
# question Krypto: it is a clipped prefix of Kryptowährung and similar words, so no single obvious target; I dropped it.
# question Kärntner: demonym/adjective from a place name; treated as place-derived and dropped.
# question LEDs: the rules do not say whether the singular LED is itself a keepable abbreviation or a specialist acronym
# question LKW: kept as an established abbreviation, but the rules do not say whether it should merge into Lastkraftwagen
# question Lab: it could be an abbreviation of Labor, but the counted forms look English (lab, labs), so I dropped it.
# question Lade: could be the noun Lade (drawer) rather than a form of laden; the tag is VERB, so merged into laden.
# question Lady: this may count as an established loan (Duden lists it); I judged it mostly English usage
# question Lebende: could stay as a nominalised-adjective noun (der Lebende) instead of merging into lebend.
# question Lebensmittelvorrat: most counts are the truncated "lebensmittel-"; kept the lemma as a valid noun.
# question Letzt: the headword is arguably letzte, not letzt. Merged to letzt.
# question Lkw: unclear whether Lastkraftwagen is listed or Lkw counts as an established abbreviation (dropped, as with Kfz)
# question Loslasse: the examples are nominalised (das Loslassen), so merge:Loslassen is arguable; I chose the verb.
# question Längere: only "seit längerem" is attested, so it may be a fixed phrase rather than an inflected adjective
# question MM: could be Millimeter, a media abbreviation or other, with no examples
# question Macbook: product name, not clearly an established loan; dropped
# question Mainzer: Demonyms can be nouns in their own right, but the rules route place-derived forms to the place-name list, so I dropped it.
# question Malteser: organisation name vs. demonym for a person from Malta; kept either way
# question Marvel: famous company, but a brand rather than an institution; dropped as an "other name"
# question Mega: could be dropped as a prefix instead; merged to mega as a colloquial adjective, which may not be on the list.
# question Metro: no examples to confirm whether it is the transit loan or the company name; I kept it.
# question Miele: brand and company names (Miele, Karstadt, Monsanto, PayPal, Pioneer, Arduino) are not clearly "famous institutions" or "other names"; I treated them as other names and dropped them.
# question MitarbeiterIn: merged to Mitarbeiterin rather than Mitarbeiter, because the counted forms are almost all feminine; this assumes Mitarbeiterin is its own lemma
# question Mod: unclear whether it is the English mod, an abbreviation, or Modus (modes). Dropped.
# question Mose: the lemma is a biblical name and also a book title (3. Mose); I treated it as a name and dropped it.
# question Mächtige: the rules don't say whether nominalised adjectives that appear only in inflected form should be kept as nouns or merged into the adjective; same for Jüngere
# question Münchener: Demonym adjectives from place names (Münchener, Basler) are not covered explicitly; I dropped them under the place-name rule.
# question Münchner: a demonym derived from München, which is not itself a place name; I kept it, but the place-name rules might be meant to cover it
# question NGOs: unclear whether NGO counts as a specialist acronym (drop) or an established abbreviation; I chose merge:NGO.
# question Ne: I assumed colloquial "ne" is an acceptable lowercase lemma; if not, drop it.
# question OG: Is Obergeschoss a listed lemma? If so, this should be merge:Obergeschoss.
# question OP: it is a common standalone word (im OP), and I assumed Operation is the listed lemma.
# question Ober: Ober (waiter) is a real noun, but the forms shown (oberen, ober) are adjectival; I merged to ober, and the dictionary adjective form is unclear.
# question Oscar: examples are mostly the first name (Oscar Wilde); kept as the award word
# question PKW: Personenkraftwagen is not necessarily on the list; I kept PKW as an established word, like Kfz
# question PR: general-use abbreviation or specialist acronym? I kept it, but it could be merged to Public Relations or dropped.
# question Pc: assumed the lemma PC exists in the list
# question Pfizer: a company name known to learners, but not an institution, so I dropped it
# question Postbank: company name; kept as a famous institution, but it may count as a brand.
# question Prager: the rules cover place names and derived demonyms only loosely; I dropped it as derived from a place name not on the list, while Norweger is kept as a demonym noun.
# question Prim: the screeners suggest Primzahl or prime, but the forms are mostly English "prime"; I dropped it because no German target is clear.
# question Python: mostly the programming language, but also the animal noun; I kept it as an established loan.
# question Quest: dropped as gaming jargon, but it may count as an established loan.
# question Quo: no standalone "Status quo" lemma is known to exist; merge target assumed
# question Raiffeisen: a person's name and a famous bank; I treated it as a name and dropped it.
# question Raiffeisenbank: generic noun for a bank type, or a company name to drop? Kept as the generic noun.
# question Ravensburger: brand (Ravensburger Spiele) or demonym; treated as a place-derived name
# question Regensburger: a demonym is a noun with its own entry, like Lehrer, but it is derived from a place name that is not on the list. I dropped it.
# question Reset: borderline English loan; kept because it is in common German use
# question Rewe: it is unclear whether a company counts as a "famous institution"; I treated this major retailer as one
# question Roma: The written form is lower-case, which could be the city Rome or the noun Roma. I kept it as the ethnonym.
# question Rostocker: it is a demonym, and the rules have no explicit ruling for demonyms; I treated it like a place name (same for Ulmer).
# question Sankt: it is a word in its own right, but it is used almost only in place names (St.)
# question Saturn: a planet is not a place name or a person, so I kept it. Venus follows the same ruling; if the rules treat planets as names, both should be dropped.
# question Schild: the forms mix in schildern; the Schild noun was kept, but the verb forms may need a separate lemma.
# question Schrei: lemma also absorbs Schreier (a separate noun); kept as Schrei.
# question Schwarze: it could be a noun in its own right (Schwarzer/Schwarze, a person), but the forms are only schwarzen, so I merged it.
# question Sek: could also stand for another word (e.g. Sekundarstufe); merged to Sekunde as the most likely.
# question Shell: computing term vs company name; I dropped it as foreign or brand, but it may count as an established loan.
# question St: unclear whether this abbreviation of Sankt/Stück should be merged or dropped. I dropped it.
# question Staatsoper: it is written generically, but the examples mostly refer to the Wiener Staatsoper; I kept it as a common noun.
# question Stack: borderline IT jargon; kept as an established loan
# question Stag: forms are mainly "stage", which may be a brand name (Stage Entertainment) or the English word; I dropped it.
# question Strick: forms are mixed between the noun Strick and the verb stricken; I kept it because it is a real noun with the most forms.
# question Stütze: only the plural-looking form stützen is attested, and it could be either the noun's plural or the verb. I merged it to the verb stützen, but the rule on forms listed only as plural does not clearly cover it.
# question Swisscom: Same company-versus-institution question as Edeka.
# question Tagesspiegel: newspaper, kept as a famous institution, but it is less clearly famous than Commerzbank or Schufa
# question Toke: Token may not be on the list as a lemma; merged anyway as the obvious target
# question UdSSR: the rules do not say whether a historical state counts as a place name or a learnable word; I treated it as a place name and dropped it.
# question Versage: Versagen (noun) and Versager (person) are both possible targets; I chose Versagen because it makes up almost all the counts.
# question Vierter: the dictionary form of the ordinal is unclear (vierte vs viert), so I used merge:vierte
# question WG: Merged to Wohngemeinschaft as an abbreviation; if WG counts as an established word in its own right, it should be keep.
# question WM: if Weltmeisterschaft is not on the list, WM is probably better kept as an established abbreviation
# question Wacker: the examples are the company name, but the lemma is also the real adjective wacker; merged to the adjective.
# question Weimarer: "Weimarer Republik" is a fixed historical term, but the word is a place-derived adjective, so I dropped it.
# question Weiter: weiters is an Austrian adverb with its own entry, so merge:weiters could also be right; I merged into weiter.
# question Wiesbadener: demonym and place adjective, dropped under the place-name rule; unclear whether demonyms count as place names.
# question Würzburger: same as Wiesbadener.
# question abgeleitet: unclear whether it has its own adjective entry; I treated it as having one.
# question admin: unclear whether Administrator or Admin is the listed lemma; I merged to Administrator.
# question anfang: the forms (anfang, anfange, anfanger) could belong to the noun Anfang or to anfangen; I chose the verb.
# question angehörig: it is a nominalised adjective (Angehöriger/Angehörige), and the rules do not say which form is the lemma; I used merge:Angehörige.
# question anpfeifen: anpfeifen is a real verb, but the forms are mostly the noun Anpfiff.
# question aufeinanderfolgen: almost all forms are the participle adjective aufeinanderfolgend, but the infinitive is a real verb, so kept
# question aufm: a colloquial contraction of auf dem. The rules do not cover it, and merging into auf would be wrong, so I dropped it.
# question auseinanderzusetzen: the verb is normally pronominal (sich auseinandersetzen), but I don't know whether that lemma exists in the list, so I merged to the plain form.
# question ausgebildet: may count as an adjective with its own entry like "ausgezeichnet" (would be keep); chose merge
# question ausgenommen: also works as a preposition or conjunction ("except"), which could justify keeping it; merged as a participle
# question ausreden: the forms mix the noun Ausrede with the verb ausreden. I merged to the noun because it is the majority.
# question auswachsen: counted forms mix the verb with the noun Auswuchs and the adjective ausgewachsen; kept as the verb
# question bad: could be the English adjective or the German noun Bad (Bad Homburg etc.); merged to Bad as the likelier reading
# question behindern: forms are mostly the adjective behinderte, but the verb is a real dictionary form, so kept
# question bekanntwerden: forms such as "wurde bekannt" are verb uses; merged into the nominalised noun Bekanntwerden.
# question ben: The forms best/ben/benen/bte/benden look like cut-off pieces of several words (e.g. üben, beben), so no single merge target is obvious.
# question berichte: could also be the plural of Bericht, but it is tagged VERB and appears only in lower case, so merged into berichten
# question bi: forms (bi, bier, bien, biem, bider) do not point to one lemma; dropped as an unidentifiable fragment
# question box: forms box and boxer may be Boxer (person or dog) rather than Box; merged to Box as the lower-case noun
# question cd: unclear whether CD is already on the list; merged assuming it is.
# question chat: the forms chat/chats/chaten could belong to Chat or chatten; I chose the noun Chat.
# question dh: abbreviation of "das heißt", but it's unclear whether "d.h." is a listed lemma to merge into; I dropped it.
# question dichter: could be the lower-case noun Dichter; tagged ADJ, so merged into dicht
# question dr: Dr could also be read as a title rather than an abbreviation of a listed word; I merged it to Doktor.
# question dritt: the ordinal's dictionary lemma could be dritte or dritter; I chose dritte.
# question durchfahren: The counted forms are mostly durchführen, so I kept durchfahren as a real verb, but the counts may belong to a different lemma.
# question düsen: noun plural Düse and verb düsen are mixed under one lemma; kept as a valid verb.
# question ebay: brand names are dropped here, but eBay is also used as a common word in German. Dropped.
# question ect: not sure "etc" is already on the list; if it isn't, drop instead.
# question eig: the forms eige and eiger suggest eigen as well as eigentlich; I merged to eigentlich.
# question escort: it may be spam-driven, and Escort may not exist as a lemma; drop is the alternative.
# question etc: I kept it as an established abbreviation; it is unclear whether "et cetera" counts as a listed word
# question facebook: Assumed Facebook is a famous company, so it should be kept once capitalised.
# question fach: it is a fragment of the -fach suffix (einfache, mehrfache), so there is no obvious target; merge:Fach is a guess
# question fahr: the forms are ambiguous between the stem of fahren and Fahrer. I chose fahren.
# question fahrt: the written forms fahrt and fahrten fit the noun Fahrt, but fahrt is also a verb form (merge:fahren); I chose the noun.
# question fehl: The counted forms are about half Fehler, so the lemma is mixed; I kept fehl as a real particle.
# question fetten: the form fette is mostly the adjective fett; kept because fetten is a real verb
# question fächern: the usage is almost entirely the adjective gefächert, but fächern is a real verb, so I kept it.
# question fönen: almost all tokens are the noun Fön (1,498 of 1,565), so the lemma may really be Fön. I kept the valid verb lemma.
# question gam: written mostly as games/game, so I merged it into Game; Gamer could also be a separate entry.
# question gedacht: forms mix denken and gedenken (gedachten sie der...), so the target is unclear
# question geimpft: Geimpfte is also a noun in its own right (der/die Geimpfte); chose the verb impfen as the dictionary form
# question gelehrt: the adjective gelehrt has its own entry, but the examples are the noun Gelehrter; merged to the noun.
# question gen: forms gen/gest/gene/gend look like fragments of different words, so I could not choose a merge target and dropped it.
# question ggf: merged on the assumption that gegebenenfalls is on the list; if it is not, this should be dropped.
# question gr: Merge target groß assumed from grer/gres/gren; could also be Größe.
# question gravieren: gravieren (to engrave) is also a real verb, but the forms are overwhelmingly gravierende.
# question größ: the forms are mostly the noun Größe, but some are the adjective groß.
# question haftungsbeschränkt: it has no verb to merge to and is a real adjective, but nearly all uses are the UG/GmbH template; I kept it, but it could be treated as boilerplate and dropped.
# question hallen: the forms are mostly the verb hallen (hall, hallt, hallte), but the lemma is also the plural of Halle; I kept it.
# question herzlichst: it might count as an adverb with its own entry (also used as a letter closing), in which case it would be keep.
# question hetzen: the noun Hetze is mixed into this lemma; it may need splitting into hetzen and Hetze
# question hihi: laughter interjection; kept as established, but could be seen as non-learnable
# question höhe: the tag is ADJ and the form hohe/höheste is possible, but höhe is most likely the lower-case noun Höhe, so I merged it there.
# question inne: innehaben and innehalten both fit; I chose innehaben.
# question inneren: lemma does not match its written forms (innert, a Swiss preposition); merged to innert
# question isch: cut-off suffix fragment (e.g. -isch) with no identifiable target word, so I dropped it rather than merging
# question jedesmal: Duden accepts it as a single word, so I kept it instead of merging into the two-word jedes Mal.
# question jo: it could be a regional particle meaning "yes" and so a keep; I dropped it as non-standard.
# question jungs: Jungs is a colloquial plural that might count as a plural with its own meaning; merged to Junge by default.
# question jährig: a suffix fragment with no obvious target lemma (the -jährig compounds), so I dropped it; the rule says fragments are merged
# question k: dropped as a stray letter, since there is no clear target (the forms kes/ke/ken suggest garbled text).
# question kommend: I kept it as an adjective with its own entry, but a dictionary may list it only as a participle of kommen.
# question kontrolliert: it may have its own adjective entry (like gemäßigt), but I treated it as a participle and merged it into kontrollieren.
# question krieg: Krieg is the main sense, but Krieger (422) is a separate noun; the lemmatiser merged them.
# question lauter: comparative of laut vs. the separate "nothing but" word; kept.
# question letzt: only the stem of letzter/letzte/letztes; merged to letzte, but the rules don't say which citation form to use.
# question letzter: all forms are letztere/letzteres/letzterer ("the latter"), which may be a separate entry from letzte; merged into letzte as the dictionary form of the inflected adjective.
# question licht: kept as the adjective, but the lower-case form could also be a noun Licht or the verb lichten; no examples to confirm.
# question lik: the forms are mostly "likes", and it is unclear whether Like is a listed lemma; I merged to Like.
# question max: unclear whether it is the abbreviation "max." for maximal or the name Max. I merged to maximal.
# question mer: the examples are missing; it may be a dialect word for "mehr" or "wir", so I dropped it.
# question mini: It could be a prefix fragment (Mini-) rather than a free adjective.
# question mittel: could be the adjective mittel (mittler, mittle) instead of the noun Mittel; merged to Mittel as the main reading
# question mod: the forms mix mod, mode and modder, so there is no single clear target, and I dropped it.
# question namen: tagged VERB, but the form is the plural of Name; merged to Name on that reading
# question ne: colloquial "ne" (eine / nicht wahr) has no clear rule; dropped as non-standard
# question nem: the rules do not cover colloquial contractions; dropped rather than merged into ein or einem
# question ner: could target "eine" or "ein"; chose "eine" since it is its own lemma and "ne/ner" is feminine-form colloquial
# question neunundzwanzig: the forms are almost all digits (29); I kept the number lemma, but the digit-only rule could apply
# question nichtmal: "nicht mal" is two words, so merging into mal is only an approximation; the alternative is to drop it
# question nunmal: the correct form "nun mal" is two words and may not exist as a lemma; merge:nun mal may need adjusting.
# question ober: the screener gives obere/oberer as the dictionary form; I chose obere
# question pag: fragment of "page", which is mostly the English word, so no German lemma to merge into
# question personal: Personal and Personaler are both nouns, and an adjective personal also exists; I picked Personaler because every example uses it.
# question pflegen: the examples are mostly the noun Pflege, but the verb lemma is valid, so I kept it instead of merging into Pflege
# question plan: an adjective plan (flat) exists, but the forms plane/plans/plan look like the noun Plan or the verb planen; I merged into Plan.
# question pm: could stand for p.m. or Pressemitteilung; dropped as ambiguous.
# question punkto: "in punkto" is a multi-word phrase and has no single-word lemma, so I merged it into Punkt.
# question red: could be the English word "red" rather than a cut-off reden; merged under the fragment rule
# question regen: forms are mostly rege (adjective) but regen also exists as a verb and as a plural; merged into reg
# question resp: I assumed respektive is on the list; if it is not, the abbreviation should be dropped.
# question riesen: indeclinable intensifier; kept as its own colloquial entry, not merged to Riese.
# question rückblicken: no dictionary verb exists; I chose merge:rückblickend, but merge:zurückblicken is the alternative.
# question sachen: Sachen can be a plural with its own meaning ("stuff"); I merged it into Sache because it is filed in lower case.
# question sch: cut-off fragment (likely from -sch adjectives such as deutsch/typisch) with no identifiable target, so dropped rather than merged
# question scheiß: it is mostly an intensifying prefix and not a clear form of the verb scheißen; drop is also possible.
# question schnellen: forms are mostly Schnelltest (a compound), merged into schnell as the closest base
# question schonmal: "schon mal" is two words, so merge:schon is only an approximation
# question schweizer: indeclinable adjective vs. demonym noun Schweizer; kept as is, but merge:Schweizer might be intended
# question selb: the dictionary form could also be derselbe; I chose selbe
# question sexkontakte: spam-like, so merging may add a junk noun if Sexkontakt is not on the list
# question shop: unclear whether Shop is already on the list; merged to Shop
# question sic: Latin insertion, could count as a foreign word rather than an established loan.
# question silber: It could instead be merged into the noun Silber. I kept it as a colour adjective.
# question skifahren: the lemmatiser may prefer "Ski fahren" as two words, and the rules do not cover that
# question spiel: forms are mostly spieler but also spieles/spieln, so the target could be spielen
# question spitzen: The forms mix the verb spitzen and the adjective spitz, so the lemma may need splitting.
# question start: the forms are ambiguous between the noun Start and the verb form of starten. I merged it to Start because starts and startes are noun genitives.
# question statten: forms stattet/stattete could belong to ausstatten or abstatten; merged to ausstatten
# question swing: forms are mostly "swinger", which may be spam; I merged to Swing as the lower-case lemma.
# question tags: adverb "tags" could instead merge into Tag; kept because Duden lists it as an adverb.
# question teen: could be an established loan (Teen); no usage examples to confirm, so dropped.
# question tim: forms are mostly "time" and "timer", which could merge into Timer, but the lemma "tim" itself is a name or fragment; I dropped it.
# question u: forms uder/uen/uste suggest it may not be only "und"; merged on the assumption it is u. = und
# question uber: could be the brand Uber, but the lower-case spelling suggests über with the umlaut stripped; merged.
# question usw: abbreviation of a multi-word phrase, so there is no single lemma to merge into; dropped
# question verdächtigen: forms are mostly the adjective verdächtig and the participle verdächtigt; I kept the verb because it is a real lemma.
# question vereinigt: nearly all uses are in the proper name Vereinigtes Königreich; I merged to vereinigen rather than dropping it as a name.
# question vergleich: could be the noun Vergleich or the verb vergleichen (the form vergleiche is ambiguous); I chose the noun
# question viert: merged to vierte, but the ordinal's dictionary form (vierte vs. vierter) is not settled by the rules
# question vierte: the dictionary form of an inflected ordinal is unclear (vierter vs. viert-); merged to the -er form, same for fünfte and sechste
# question viertel: lower-case fraction/time word; the rules on lower-case nouns could point to merge:Viertel, but the usage here is adverbial/numeral, so I kept it.
# question vorgegeben: it could be an adjective with its own entry; I treated it as a participle.
# question vornherein: no single-word lemma exists for the phrase "von vornherein"; merged to the phrase, which may not be a valid lemma.
# question wei: the garbled forms most likely come from weiß, but this is not certain.
# question weiden: the written forms mix noun (Weide) and verb (weiden) forms; the examples are all the noun, so merged to Weide.
# question weinen: the screener's note says the 'Weine' examples are the noun plural of Wein, but the written forms are mostly verb forms
# question weis: forms also include weise/weisen (weise adj, weisen verb), so merge:wissen fits only part of the evidence.
# question wildern: the counted forms are mostly the adjective wilder, so the verb is kept and the adjective count is not reassigned.
# question willen: tagged VERB, but it is really the preposition in "um ... willen". I kept it.
# question wuerde: the lemmatiser may file würde under werden, so the correct target might be merge:werden
# question wund: forms include wunderer/wundere, which belong to wunder, not wund; kept.
# question wärs: a contraction is not covered explicitly by the rules; I merged to sein as a conjugated form.
# question zb: unclear whether an abbreviation of "zum Beispiel" counts as an abbreviation of a listed word; dropped
# question zuviel: no single lemma for "zu viel"; merged into viel as the closest listed word
# question zweitgrößte: the dictionary form zweitgrößt is not a headword; merging is a best guess.
# question Ähnliche: lemma is written only as "ähnliches" (nominalised); chose keep as a noun in its own right, otherwise merge:ähnlich
# question Äußere: only lower-case "äußere" is attested and no example exists, so it may be the adjective (merge:äußer) rather than the noun
# question überragen: most tokens are the adjective überragend, but the infinitive is a real verb lemma, so I kept it; the rules do not say whether überragend should get its own entry
# question AG/FH: merge assumes Aktiengesellschaft and Fachhochschule are on the list; if they are not, should the abbreviation be kept instead?
# question Glut, schnellen, ausreden, gravieren, anpfeifen, sachverständig, Ober: these were kept as real words, but most of their counts belong to another word (Gluten, Schnelltest, Ausrede, gravierend, Anpfiff, Sachverständiger, ober). Should those counts be re-lemmatised or split?
# question Hz, Sek, OG: the rule merges abbreviations of listed words, but I cannot see whether Hertz, Sekunde or Obergeschoss are listed. Should unambiguous unit symbols (Hz→Hertz) merge when the target is listed?
# question Jakobsweg, Pfälzer: do named routes count as place names, and do demonyms and place-derived adjectives (Pfälzer, Berliner) count as names?
# question Maxi, Krypto: should established clippings be kept as their own lemma, or merged into the full word? Is Kryptowährung on the list?
# question PR, OP, BH, Lkw: lexicalised abbreviations were kept here, but Kfz was apparently excluded. Should Kfz/Pkw be kept as well for consistency?
# question Pariser: are demonym adjectives and nouns from listed places (Pariser, Berliner, Wiener) to be kept as words?
# question Swisscom, Wacker: do large companies count as "famous institutions"? Is regional fame (Swisscom in Switzerland) enough?
# question aufeinanderfolgen, komplizieren, verheeren: most forms are the participle adjectives (aufeinanderfolgend, kompliziert, verheerend); should those forms be moved to the adjective lemma, or stay under the valid verb?
# question aufm, ists, kannste: please confirm that colloquial contractions merge to their base word (auf, sein, können), as with gabs/wars.
# question einem/eines: confirm that inflected article forms (einem, eines, also einer, einen) follow the ein/eine own-lemma convention rather than merging into ein
# question fach, Fu, ie: fragments are to be merged, but these have no single obvious target; is drop correct, or should they be held?
# question flirt: the lemma reads as the noun Flirt, but most counted forms are the verb flirten. Should the counts be split between the two?
# question flöten, motorisieren, verträumen, wildern, erbleichen, kranken, gelehrt, Stütze, Kollage: each lemma is kept or merged on its own merits, but most of its counts belong to another word (Flöte, motorisiert, verträumt, wild, erblich, krank, Gelehrter, Kollagen). Should those counts be reassigned, and should verträumt, Gelehrter and Kollagen be added if they are missing?
# question forsch, Drei, angehörig, Kehl: each lemma is decided on its own reading, but most counted forms belong to another word (forschen, Dreier, Angehörige(r), Kehle). Should those counts be reassigned, or those words added, if they are not already listed?
# question ner, gabs: I settled contractions as merge to the host word (ner→eine, nem→ein, gabs/wars/ists→verb, kannste→können, ichs→ich, aufm→auf); please confirm that earlier drops of nem/ists/ichs/kannste/aufm should be redone as merges.
# question verspäten, zuvorkommen, weiden: these lemmas mix a real verb with forms of another word (the participle adjective verspätet/zuvorkommend, or the noun Weide). Should such lemmas be split rather than kept or merged whole?
# question versuch, bad, FR: there are no usable examples, so lower-case noun vs imperative, English word or abbreviation was judged from the forms and tags only. Please confirm.
# question vierte/fünfte/sechste: confirm the -e form (vierte) as the single ordinal target, so Vierter, viert and dritt merge into it
# question wg: wg is ambiguous between WG (Wohngemeinschaft) and wg. (wegen), and the sample has only one example. Should WG be its own lemma as an everyday word?
# question zigeunern: the rules don't cover offensive or slur-derived words that are genuinely used; should they be dropped as a class?
# question zuvorkommen: most occurrences (zuvorkommend/-e/-en) are the separate adjective zuvorkommend; should those forms be split off into their own zuvorkommend lemma (added if not already listed), since keep/merge cannot express a split?
# question APIs: merging into API assumes API itself is kept; as a specialist acronym it could be dropped.
# question AStA: abbreviation of a specific institution type (student union), but not obviously a famous institution; dropped as a specialist acronym
# question Achso: interjection with no lowercase lemma rule; I chose merge:achso.
# question Airbnb: a brand name that could count as a famous institution, but I treated it as an ordinary name.
# question Amor: kept as a common noun, but it could count as a mythological name (drop).
# question Appartment: Apartment and Appartement are both valid targets; I chose Apartment
# question Appenzeller: the examples are mostly surnames, but the demonym/cheese reading is a common noun, so I kept it
# question Auslese: the forms are mostly the nominalised verb Auslesen, but Auslese is a real noun, so I kept it
# question Azor: Azoren is a place name not on the list, so the target will probably be dropped; I merged the fragment as the rules say.
# question Baht: foreign currency name; unclear whether it counts as an established loan. I chose keep.
# question Blond: the counted text is lower-cased, so adjective vs. noun use can't be told; kept on the basis of the noun example.
# question Blu: fragment with no examples; could be Blues or a brand (Blu-ray), so I dropped it as unidentifiable
# question Buffett: examples mix the surname with the misspelling of Buffet; dropped as a name
# question Castor: it is a brand/name but also a common noun for the container; I kept it.
# question Champagne: it could also be treated as a region name (drop), but the examples use it as the drink.
# question Chemtrails: it may be a legitimate plural-only term; I merged it to the singular.
# question Cruiser: could be an established loan (bike type), but the evidence points to brand use; dropped.
# question Dat: written as dates/dat/daten, so it could be Date or Daten; ambiguous, so I dropped it.
# question Drift: the forms mix the noun with driften (verb); I kept it as the noun loan
# question Dschungelcamp: TV show name or common noun? I chose keep.
# question Döbel: most forms are the place name Döbeln, but Döbel the fish is a valid noun, so kept.
# question Dürr: tagged NOUN, with surname examples. The lower-case adjective dürr may be mixed in, so merge:dürr is possible; I dropped it.
# question Eiffelturm: it is a landmark with a plural (Eiffeltürme) rather than a plain place name, so the place-name rule may not apply
# question Eingreifen: the forms are mostly lower-case "eingreifen", but the examples show nominalised use; I kept it as a noun like Essen.
# question Energiespar: the examples use the nominalised Energiesparen, so merge:Energiesparen could also fit; I chose the verb.
# question Erasmus: a programme name that is also a personal name; I treated it as a famous institution.
# question Ernste: it is unclear whether this is the noun Ernst (allen Ernstes) or the nominalised adjective (nichts Ernstes); I chose merge:Ernst.
# question Ether: Ether is also the chemical and crypto term, so it may deserve its own entry rather than a merge into Äther.
# question Ev: could be ev. (evangelisch) or e.V.; I dropped it as unclear
# question Fahr: could be a fragment of Fahrt or fahren; the imperative form fahr suggests fahren
# question Finanztest: it is a well-known publication, but I treated it as a product name rather than a famous institution
# question Flensburger: a place-name derivative that is also a brand (Flensburger Pilsener); the rules don't cover derivatives, so I dropped it as a place-linked word.
# question Fon: could be a variant of Phon (unit of loudness), but the counted forms look like brand or fragment use; dropped
# question Forbes: a magazine/company name; I treated it as a famous institution, unlike Wirecard and Märklin.
# question Freske: Freske is a rare variant of Fresko, and the forms are mostly the plural fresken, so I merged it into Fresko.
# question Fritzbox: brand name, but also a generic everyday word for a router; I dropped it as a brand.
# question Frontex: an EU agency; kept as a famous institution, but "famous" is borderline.
# question Fünfter: the base form of an ordinal (fünfte vs fünft) is unclear; chose fünfte as the attested form
# question Gamescom: trade fair, not clearly a famous institution; I dropped it.
# question Gangbang: vulgar and spam-heavy contexts, kept only because the rules keep established loans
# question Ge: fragment with no obvious target, so I dropped it although fragments are normally merged
# question GewO: drop assumes Gewerbeordnung is not on the list; if it is, this should be merge:Gewerbeordnung
# question Gezeit: the lemma is singular but the text is nearly all plural Gezeiten; merge:Gezeiten might be the better lemma.
# question Gmail: a brand name that is also a common product noun; I dropped it as an "other name".
# question Graphik: I kept it as an accepted variant, although it duplicates Grafik if that is also on the list.
# question Haha: interjection filed under a capital; I merged to lower-case haha, but it may not be a list lemma and could be a drop.
# question Hanse: proper-noun use dominates (Hanse Merkur), but the historical league is a common noun, so I kept it.
# question Heilbronner: the rules do not say whether place-derived adjectives or inhabitant forms count as place names, and I dropped it, as I did Passauer
# question Herold: examples mix the common noun (herald) with a surname; I kept it as the common noun.
# question Hobbit: a fictional race, but a standard dictionary noun, so I did not treat it as a name.
# question Hut: forms are mixed with hüten (verb); I kept Hut, but the hüten forms may belong to a separate lemma.
# question Inclusive: "All Inclusive" is a common German loan phrase, but the single word is not a lemma; dropped
# question Infizierte: kept as a nominalised adjective in its own right; if the rules treat it as an inflected form, it should merge to infiziert
# question Inka: could also be the female given name; the no-example evidence does not settle it
# question Irre: written forms are lower-case adjective forms (irren, irrer, irres), but the examples are the noun der/die Irre; kept as a noun.
# question Jacuzzi: also a brand name, but kept because it works as a generic noun; this is not consistent with Airbnb and Powerpoint.
# question Johanniter: could be treated as an organisation name (drop); kept as a famous institution
# question Jährige: no standalone adjective jährig exists outside compounds; merged into jährig as the nearest base.
# question Kaschmir: it is both a place name (drop) and a fabric (common noun); kept for the fabric sense.
# question Keno: it is a game as well as a given name; I dropped it as a name-like proper noun.
# question Ki: merged to KI, but if KI counts as a specialist acronym it should be dropped
# question Kirsch: forms include kirsche and kirschs; it could be the spirit (Kirsch) or a surname, and I dropped it as a surname.
# question Kleben: same issue as Töten, a nominalised infinitive with no separate noun meaning
# question Klett: a publisher is not clearly a famous institution; I dropped it as a brand name.
# question Kommende: no examples, so unclear whether it is the nominalised noun (die Kommenden) or the adjective kommend; merged to kommend
# question Konservativer: the lemma is an inflected form, but the example is the noun (die Konservativen); merged to the noun Konservative, though konservativ may be intended.
# question Kung: the fragment of Kung-Fu, but I'm not sure Kung-Fu is on the list as a lemma; if it isn't, drop instead
# question Kärcher: brand name that is also used generically; I kept it, but the rules don't settle brand-turned-common-noun cases
# question Lateinische: the example is the nominalised language name (das Lateinische); I chose Latein over lateinisch.
# question Lehne: Lehne (backrest) is a real noun, but the counted forms are lehne/lehnen, so I merged into lehnen.
# question Limburger: it could be the cheese name (a noun), but the examples are all the demonym, so I dropped it.
# question Liquids: the vape sense of "Liquid" may not be a listed lemma; merged assuming it is.
# question Magnum: the examples are brand uses (ice cream, engine), so I dropped it, but Magnum is also a general word for a large bottle.
# question Mint: German uses "mint" as a colour adjective, but the evidence shows English or brand usage, so I dropped it.
# question Nahost: region name rather than a place name; dropped under the "any place name you still see" rule.
# question Neger: it is a real dictionary word but offensive; the rules don't say whether such words belong in the learnable set, so I kept it.
# question Nö: it could be kept as its own interjection lemma; I merged it into the lower-case nö.
# question Oberhaus: a real noun (upper house), but most counts are the place Oberhausen; dropped.
# question Oberpfälzer: demonym adjective from a region name; no rule covers it, dropped as place-derived.
# question Olymp: also a common noun (figurative "Olymp"), but treated as a place name per the rules
# question Olympus: brand name, not a famous institution, so treated as an other name; a loan-word reading would keep it
# question Orga: a colloquial clipping with its own dictionary entry in some sources, so keep may also be defensible
# question Ostseeküste: a common-noun compound that names a specific coast; I treated it as a place name and dropped it.
# question PAYBACK: it is a famous German brand, and the rules cover institutions but not brands; I dropped it.
# question Par: unclear whether this is the German golf noun Par (keep) or foreign/garbled; dropped
# question Pflegende: kept as a nominalised participle noun; Älteste is treated the same way, but the rules do not say whether such forms should merge to pflegen or alt
# question Pie: English loan with weak establishment in German; kept as an established food loan
# question Pinot: cut off from "Pinot Noir", but no multiword lemma exists to merge into; dropped as a foreign wine name.
# question Playboy: it could count as a brand name, but I kept it as an established loan noun.
# question Polier: Polier (foreman) is a real noun, but the forms are almost all polieren, so I merged into polieren.
# question Powerpoint: a software brand, not clearly a famous institution or an established loan.
# question Radel: examples show the nominalised "das Radeln", which could instead be merge:Radeln; I chose the verb.
# question Rapunzel: also a plant name and a brand, but I treated it as a fairy-tale character name
# question Ref: unclear whether it clips Referenz, Referee or Referent, so no single merge target; dropped.
# question Referrer: it is an English word, but it is in established German web use; kept as a loan.
# question Reif: the noun Reif (frost, ring) exists, but all counted forms are the adjective reif, so I merged.
# question Reseller: is it an established loan or a business-jargon English word? Decided keep.
# question Riester: it could count as a lexicalised term for the pension (Riester-Rente), but I treated it as a person name.
# question Roche: company name; the rules cover "famous institutions" and "other names" but not companies, so I dropped it
# question Rolex: brand name, not clearly a famous institution; dropped as other name.
# question Rotary: famous institution (keep) or ordinary organisation name (drop)?
# question Saarbrücker: a demonym adjective derived from a place name, which the rules don't cover explicitly; I treated it like the place name.
# question Schenker: surname/company use exists, but the examples show the common noun (giver), so I kept it.
# question Schlumpf: character name, but Schlümpfe is a widely known common word; I dropped it as a name
# question Schreibe: could be the noun Schreiben (letter) rather than the verb; I merged to the verb.
# question Seidenstraße: a historical route rather than a place, but treated as a place name per the rules
# question Shar: Share may itself be a borderline lemma, but the rules say to merge fragments rather than drop them.
# question Sildenafil: I dropped it as specialist, but Paracetamol is kept as a household generic. Please confirm where the line falls.
# question Sixt: a famous company, but the rules say other names drop; I treated it as not a famous institution.
# question Soli: could be the clipped tax term (der Soli) rather than the plural of Solo; merged to Solo as the plural reading fits the lower-case forms.
# question Sp: no obvious target word for this fragment; I dropped it instead of merging
# question Spielende: could be read as a nominalised participle (players) or the noun Spielende (end of game); kept.
# question Spike: mixed use as a character name, the loan Spikes (shoes) and the verb spiken; kept as an established loan
# question Stärkere: it is a nominalised comparative (der Stärkere) but the counted forms are mostly adjectival; merged into stark.
# question Suhrkamp: a publisher is neither a place nor a person, so I treated it as a famous institution.
# question Surf: it could be a cut-off form of surfen, but there are no examples, so I treated it as an English word.
# question Sylvester: possible variant of Silvester (New Year's Eve), but all examples are the personal name, so I dropped it.
# question Tauber: the form is lower-case "tauber" 739 times, which could be the adjective taub, but all examples are the river Tauber, so I dropped it as a proper noun.
# question Tauer: if Tauern should count as a learnable noun (mountain range) rather than a place name, it would be keep
# question TikTok: brand name; I treated it as an established loan and kept it, but the rules don't say how to handle brands
# question Tr: forms tr/tren could be Träne, Trend and others; no obvious target, so dropped.
# question Trad: Trader and Trade are separate nouns, but the lemma is a truncated fragment and most forms are "Trades", so I merged into Trade.
# question Trierer: could be an inhabitant noun, but it is derived from a place name not on the list, so dropped
# question Tumblr: a well-known platform, but not an institution in the sense of the rules, so I dropped it as a brand name.
# question Tun: I treated this as a nominalised noun like Essen and kept it, but the forms (tuns, tunes, tunen) are odd
# question Tusch: forms are mixed with Tusche (ink); I kept Tusch as its own word, but the Tusche forms may need separating.
# question Täufer: surname Täufer appears in some examples, but the common noun dominates; I chose keep.
# question Töten: nominalised infinitive (as in "Lizenz zum Töten"); the rules do not say whether it counts as a noun like Essen
# question Tübinger: the form is a place-derived adjective or noun, so I dropped it as a place name; a reviewer could instead merge it away or keep it as an adjective.
# question Ultrabook: generic product term or brand name? Decided drop.
# question Unbewusst: the lemma is not the noun form Unbewusste, and the examples are all nominalised; I kept it, but merge:unbewusst is also defensible.
# question Univers: forms are "universe/universen"; the English "Universe" may be a title, merged to Universum anyway
# question Unix: product name for a widely known OS; rules cover only institutions and other names, so I treated it as a name and dropped it
# question Untere: the adjective's dictionary form is unclear (unter vs. unterer); I used unter.
# question Vierte: only "vierten" is attested, so the dictionary form (viert vs. vierter) is unclear; I merged to viert
# question Viewer: I kept it as a listed loan, but the screener judged it not established.
# question Violett: colour noun (keep) or adjective violett (merge)? Counted text mostly lower-case, so the noun reading is uncertain.
# question Vogue: a famous magazine, but possibly just a brand name, which would be drop.
# question Vorhinein: only occurs in "im Vorhinein"; I kept it as a fixed noun rather than treating it as a fragment.
# question Walliser: a demonym from a place name; the rules cover only the place name itself, so I dropped it.
# question Walser: the people (a common noun) or a surname? Both appear in the examples; I chose drop as a name.
# question Walt: written forms are mostly the name Walt, but walten (41) is a verb, so merge:walten was possible
# question Warschauer: derived adjective rather than a plain place name; treated as a name-derived form and dropped.
# question Wirtschaftswoche: a magazine title is not clearly a "famous institution"; kept as a major national publication.
# question Wohn: fragment of Wohn- compounds with no single dictionary form; dropped rather than merged, though the rules say fragments are merged
# question Zapfe: the written form is only "zapfen", which could be the verb or the noun Zapfen; I chose the noun on the basis of the example.
# question Zeitlang: only occurs in "eine Zeitlang", which could be treated as a fragment of "eine Zeit lang"; kept as an adverbial noun.
# question Zumba: kept as a loan, though it is a trademark.
# question Zuviel: surface forms are mostly lower-case adverbial "zuviel"; I kept it because the examples show a nominalised noun.
# question Zwanziger: could also be the surname of the DFB president; I kept it as the common noun.
# question Zweit: fragment of zweit-; I chose zweite as the closest word, but the target is unclear
# question abpfeifen: the lemma mixes verb forms with the noun Abpfiff (581 of 679 tokens); I kept it as a valid verb, but Abpfiff may need its own entry.
# question android: could be merged to Androide as an adapted loan, but the counted text is mostly the Android brand; dropped
# question anno: Latin word, kept because of fixed German use ("anno dazumal"); could count as a foreign word to drop
# question ansich: target "an sich" is a two-word phrase; unclear whether the list holds it as one entry
# question aufgeladen: "emotional aufgeladen" is common and may count as an adjective with its own entry; I merged it into aufladen.
# question ausm: a colloquial contraction rather than a broken lemma; I dropped it instead of merging to aus.
# question beauftragt: could be kept as adjective or merged into the noun Beauftragte; chose the noun from the examples.
# question besaufen: most tokens are besoffen (an adjective with its own entry); I kept the lemma besaufen and did not merge.
# question beschenkt: could instead be merged to beschenken; I chose the nominalised noun Beschenkte.
