# question beschämen: most uses are beschämend (an adjective with its own entry), so it could be merged there. I kept it because the verb is valid.
# question bevollmächtigt: the correct lemma could be Bevollmächtigter (masculine) or Bevollmächtigte (feminine); chose Bevollmächtigte, since the feminine forms are the most frequent in the examples.
# question bio: could be seen as a lower-case noun (merge:Bio), but it is also an indeclinable adjective, so I kept it.
# question bisserl: dialect word, not a broken lemma; kept rather than merged into bisschen.
# question cam: could be read as an abbreviation of Kamera/Webcam, but the text shows English use
# question city: is it a loan or just English text? Decided drop.
# question content: the rules do not say whether it is an established loan (keep) or a lower-case noun filed wrongly; I merged to Content.
# question darüberhinaus: same issue, the target "darüber hinaus" is two words.
# question date: no examples; assumed English text, dropped rather than merged into daten.
# question designieren: usage is almost entirely the adjective designiert, which is also a dictionary entry. I kept the verb lemma, but merge:designiert is arguable.
# question destilliert: it may count as an adjective with its own dictionary entry, which would be a keep. I merged to the verb destillieren.
# question dos: forms dose/dosen suggest Dose, but the lemma itself is English "dos"; dropped rather than merged.
# question erkrankt: it may be a nominalised adjective (der Erkrankte), which would be a keep; I merged it to erkranken because the lemma is lower-case.
# question erster: lemma is really the "der erstere" pronoun-like adjective; merged to erstere, but could be keep as its own entry
# question familiengeführte: no infinitive exists to merge to, so I chose the base adjective familiengeführt; it could instead be kept as an adjective with its own entry.
# question feinschleifen: the counted forms are mostly Feinschliff, with some feingeschliffen/feinschleifen; I merged to Feinschliff as the dominant form.
# question fing: the counted forms are mostly "finger" (319) and only 161 "fing"; I merged to fangen, but the lemma may be mixed with Finger.
# question flauen: the forms are mostly the noun Flaute, but flaute can also be the verb (abflauen); I chose the noun
# question freistehen: the counted text is mostly the adjective freistehend; it is kept as the verb, but merge:freistehend may be intended
# question fördernd: "fördernde Mitglieder" is a fixed use, but I could not confirm that fördernd has its own entry, so I merged it. trauernd is kept on the same basis because of Trauernde.
# question fürn: contraction of für den/ein; unclear whether merge into für or drop
# question gangbang: could count as an established loan, but I dropped it as a non-German vulgar term
# question garnichts: could instead stay as a variant of "gar nichts"; merged into nichts since no multiword lemma is possible.
# question gedanken: Gedanken could be treated as a plural-only noun with its own meaning; chose merge:Gedanke
# question gehend: the tokens are probably from compounds such as weitgehend; merged to gehen, but may be better dropped.
# question gekürzt: participle-adjective with its own dictionary entry (like bekannt) or merge to kürzen? I chose merge.
# question gesagt: "das Gesagte" is a nominalised participle; I merged it to sagen, but it could be argued as its own entry
# question geschwächt: same issue as gekürzt; I merged it into schwächen.
# question gespalten: unsure whether this participle has its own adjective entry; I kept it
# question getarnt: a participle adjective that may have its own entry; merged to tarnen as the lemma isn't clearly lexicalised
# question getönt: participle that may or may not count as an adjective with its own entry; kept because it is common in product text.
# question gewicht: the screener tags it as a verb form of gewichten, but the forms gewicht, gewichte and gewichtes read more like the noun Gewicht, so I merged it there.
# question griech: could be Grieche or griechisch; chose the adjective because "griech" is the stem of griechisch
# question ham: could be read as dialect haben/ihm (merge:haben) but the forms hamer/hams are too ambiguous, so dropped
# question hmmm: could instead merge to "hm" if that interjection is on the list
# question ick: dialect form of ich; the rules do not say whether to merge or drop. I chose drop.
# question imMehr: it could be "immer mehr" or "im Mehr", so no merge target is obvious. I dropped it.
# question infinit: German adjective (infinite verb forms) exists, but nearly all tokens are the English "Infinite" in product names, so I dropped it
# question infos: merge target could be Info or Information; chose Info as the singular of the form filed.
# question inkl: assumed inklusive is on the list
# question innenliegend: it is a participle-like adjective with no obvious verb, so I kept it
# question ka: it could also be merged to kein (dialect) or be the chat abbreviation kA; I dropped it.
# question karten: could be lower-cased plural of Karte; tagged VERB with kartet present, so kept as a verb.
# question lag: lemma is garbled across Lager (354), Lage (142), lagem and lages; I merged to the majority form, but Lage is also plausible.
# question li: forms like lis/lien/lider cannot be traced to one word, so I dropped it rather than guess a merge.
# question logo: the interjection "logo" (colloquial) is possible, but the plural logos points to the noun Logo.
# question lt: forms lt/lten/ltere suggest a dropped ä (alt/älter), but the source is not certain.
# question lud: could be laden or einladen (many contexts are "lud ... ein"); merged into laden.
# question mark: forms could be Marke (brand) or Mark (currency, marrow); I chose Marke because marke/marker dominate.
# question mchte: the real word is möchte, but it is itself a form of mögen, so the target could be möchten or mögen
# question mei: forms like meien and meier suggest fragments of mein or the surname Meier, while mei is a Bavarian interjection; I dropped it.
# question nichtmehr: the target "nicht mehr" is two words, so it may not work as a lemma.
# question nocheinmal: the target "noch einmal" is two words and may not be a valid single lemma; drop could be the alternative.
# question pol: it could also be Pol, Polen or the verb polen; I chose Pol as the most direct reading of the lemma.
# question post: forms mix post/poster/gepost, so no clear target
# question pulen: lemma is a real verb (pulen) but nearly all counts are Pult; merged into Pult.
# question rde: broken encoding of würde, which is a conjugated form; I merged to the dictionary form werden rather than to würde.
# question regierend: this participle may count as an adjective with its own entry (kept), but the rules do not settle it
# question ren: forms ren/rene/rend/rende look like a fragment of many verbs, so no merge target is obvious; dropped
# question retour: examples are spam text, but the word itself is in dictionaries; kept.
# question rlich: the missing letters are not recoverable (ä, ö or ü plus other letters), so I dropped it rather than merged it.
# question scheiss: the examples are mostly noun uses (der Scheiß) but it is tagged ADJ; I chose merge:Scheiß, though merge:scheiß (adjective) is also possible
# question schlampen: forms are a mix of noun (Schlampe) and verb (schlampen, geschlampt); I chose the noun because it has the most forms.
# question schlitzen: lemma is a valid verb but the counted forms are mostly the noun Schlitze; I kept it, though a Schlitz entry may be needed
# question schul: forms are mixed (Schule, Schulden, Schulen, schuler), so the lemma is a fragment; I merged to Schule as the dominant form, but Schulden is a separate plural-only noun.
# question selbstgebaut: no verb "selbstbauen" exists to merge into, so I kept the participle adjective; it could be argued either way.
# question siehts: contraction could belong to sehen, but all examples are "aussehen"; I chose aussehen.
# question sms: kept as an established abbreviation, but a lower-case form may need merging to an upper-case SMS entry if one exists
# question sond: sonder is itself a marginal word, so the target lemma may not exist on the list
# question spachteln: counts mix noun (Spachtel, 404) and verb forms; kept as the verb, and the noun Spachtel may need a separate entry
# question stet: kept as a dictionary adjective, but it could also be read as a form of stetig
# question strand: could be a form of the verb stranden rather than the noun Strand; I chose Strand because the lower-case forms are mostly the noun.
# question stream: the forms mix noun (Stream, Streamer) and verb (streamen); I chose the noun.
# question studierend: I chose merge:Studierende (nominalised noun) over merge:studieren, given the examples.
# question trauernd: also used as the noun Trauernde; merged to trauern.
# question traumatisiert: the lemma is tagged VERB, but Duden lists it as an adjective, so I kept it.
# question turteln: the frequency is almost entirely the English "Turtle" in brand names, but the lemma itself is a real German verb, so I kept it.
# question twitter: the brand name and the verb twittern are mixed, so I merged it into the verb.
# question unterlagen: it could be the plural noun Unterlagen (documents), but it is lower-case and tagged VERB, so I treated it as a form of unterliegen.
# question verlobt: the forms are noun uses, but verlobt is also an adjective with its own entry; I merged to Verlobte because the evidence is nominal.
# question vermindert: unclear whether the adjective has its own entry; merged to vermindern
# question versch: the target verschieden is a guess from the abbreviation, and the 4 counts of verschr are unexplained
# question verwaisen: nearly all uses are the adjective verwaist, but the verb lemma is valid, so kept.
# question vollgepackt: it may count as an adjective with its own entry (like ausgezeichnet), which would make it keep
# question vorlagen: could be the plural of Vorlage or the past of vorliegen; I chose Vorlage because the tokens are lower-case and the form is ambiguous
# question vors: contractions are not explicitly covered by the rules, so I kept it as a standard form
# question waer: the real word is wäre, which is a form of sein, not its own lemma; I merged it to sein. Wär got the same decision.
# question war: forms ware/warden/warem/wares/warer could be Ware, wahr or wäre with a lost umlaut; there are no examples, so no target is clear and I dropped it
# question watt: the written forms (watt, watte, watter) could also belong to Watte. I chose Watt.
# question wechsel: could be the noun Wechsel or the imperative of wechseln; I followed the VERB tag and the form wechselden.
# question wellen: the forms (welle, gewellt, wellen) mix the noun Welle, the adjective gewellt and a rare verb wellen, so the target is unclear.
# question werte: could be the plural noun Werte (filed as VERB, so merged to werten)
# question wett: "wett" could be the adjective in "wett sein", but the forms point to the verb wetten, so I merged it there
# question whatsapp: the rules do not say whether a common brand-turned-verb is an "established loan"; I dropped it as a brand name.
# question wi: the forms are mixed (wi, Wien, wier), so there is no single target; I dropped it.
# question wiedermal: the correct form "wieder mal" is two words, so I merged it into the single lemma wieder.
# question willigen: forms like willige/willigt/willigte could come from einwilligen, bewilligen or others, so I dropped it as an unclear fragment
# question wohlgefühlt: unclear whether the list's lemma is "wohlfühlen" or "sich wohlfühlen"; I followed the sich-verb convention.
# question wre: broken encoding of wäre, which is a conjugated form; I merged to the dictionary form sein rather than to wäre.
# question zumute: listed as an adverb in dictionaries but only occurs as part of "zumute sein"; kept
# question zunichte: same case as zunutze, merged into "zunichte machen".
# question zunutze: no single-word target exists; I merged it into the phrase "sich zunutze machen", which may not be a lemma.
# question zusteigen: most forms (zustieg, zustiege) belong to the noun Zustieg, but the lemma itself is a valid verb, so I kept it.
# question zutage: a fragment of zutage treten/fördern, but a valid adverb in its own right, so I kept it.
# question zutun: it is both the noun Zutun (ohne Zutun) and a misspelling of "zu tun", and the text mixes the two.
# question zuwenig: "zu wenig" is not a single lemma; I merged into wenig
# question zweigeteilt: I assumed it has its own adjective entry. If not, no obvious dictionary verb exists to merge it into.
# question zwängen: the forms mix the plural Zwänge with forms of the real verb zwängen (gezwängt, zwängt, zwängte); I chose Zwang by majority, but the verb may need its own lemma.
# question Älteste: only the form "ältesten" was counted; merged to alt, but it may be a noun in its own right (der Älteste)
# question äusser: the adjective äußer- is attributive-only; I chose "äußere" as the dictionary form.
# question äußere: "äußer-" has no standalone dictionary form, so it is unclear whether to keep it or merge it into a base; kept.
# question überein: it is a fragment of verbs such as übereinstimmen, with no obvious single target, so I dropped it rather than merging.
# question Fidel: when a name homographs a real adjective (fidel) and the name dominates the examples, should the lemma be dropped or merged into the adjective?
# question Kata: where is the line between established loans and specialist loans (martial arts, hobby terms)?
# question Lehe: if Lehen is not itself a listed lemma, should this merge create it, or should the lemma drop as an unlisted place name?
# question Unbewusst, trauernd: when the forms are mostly a nominalised adjective or participle (des Unbewussten, Trauernde) but the lemma lacks the -e, should they merge to the adjective or verb, or move to a noun lemma (Unbewusste, Trauernde)?
# question Zweit, Vierte: ordinal lemma convention unclear (zweite/vierte vs zweit/viert); I used the -te form, so please confirm which the lemmatiser uses
# question social: should English adjectives used only inside English phrases (social media) be dropped, or merged into the German cognate?
# question weils, siehts: should clitic contractions (weil's, sieht's) merge to the host word, or be dropped as colloquial?
# question zutun, Achso: fused spellings (zutun = zu tun or Zutun; achso = ach so) have mixed or multi-word readings; is merging into the main word (tun, ach) the intended handling?
# question Abg: Abgeordneter is not confirmed to be on the list, so the merge target is a guess.
# question Affiliat: the target Affiliate is an unadapted English loan, so it may not count as established; merged on the assumption that it does.
# question Ar: it is a real metric unit in the dictionary, but the lemma looks noisy (aren, ares). I dropped it.
# question Arabi: the rules say to merge a cut-off fragment into its word, but Arabien is a place name that may not be on the list; I dropped it.
# question Ascorbinsäure: the rules don't say whether a c/k spelling variant counts as a misspelling; I kept it as an accepted variant.
# question Athener: a demonym derived from a place name; I treated it as a noun in its own right and kept it.
# question Attac: unclear whether a well-known activist organisation counts as a "famous institution" (keep) or an "other name" (drop); I dropped it.
# question Ausbleibe: the written form is the nominalised infinitive "(das) Ausbleiben"; I merged to the verb, but it could stay as a noun.
# question BVerwG: it abbreviates Bundesverwaltungsgericht, which is not on the list, so I treated it as a specialist acronym and dropped it
# question Bange: "keine Bange" and "Angst und Bange" could count as a noun, but the counted forms are the adjective bange, so merged.
# question Barmer: a major insurer, so possibly a famous institution; I dropped it as a company name.
# question Bayreuther: it appears mostly in "Bayreuther Festspiele", a famous institution, but the lemma itself is a place-derived adjective, so I dropped it.
# question Begünstigte: it could be merged to begünstigen or begünstigt, but I treated it as a noun like Angestellte and kept it.
# question Benehme: could merge to the verb benehmen, but the example is the noun Benehmen, so I chose the noun.
# question Bergischen: it could instead be merged to the adjective bergisch, but I treated it as part of a regional place name.
# question Bestehende: could be read as a noun in its own right (das Bestehende); no examples, chose merge:bestehend
# question BfArM: a federal agency, but I treated it as a specialist acronym rather than a famous institution
# question Bilderberger: could count as a famous institution (keep), but I treated it as a name derived from the place Bilderberg and dropped it.
# question Bong: the botanical-authority use and the water-pipe noun are mixed in one lemma
# question Bootloader: may count as a specialist term rather than an established loan, kept
# question Bundesrechtsanwaltskammer: institution is official but not famous to a learner; dropped as a non-famous institution
# question Büffet: valid Duden variant of Buffet; kept rather than merged.
# question Cd: the target CD may not exist as a lemma; the rules don't say whether an everyday abbreviation is kept or dropped.
# question Christuskirche: it is a church name, but not a famous institution; I dropped it.
# question Creditreform: a well-known German credit agency; unclear whether it counts as a famous institution (dropped as a company name)
# question Datacenter: English compound; kept as an established loan, though Rechenzentrum is the German equivalent
# question Deuter: a real agent noun (interpreter) exists, but the evidence is almost entirely the backpack brand.
# question Diclofenac: the rules do not say whether a generic drug name counts as a name or as an established term; I kept it.
# question Diss: could be a clipping of Dissertation or of English "diss"; no usable context, so dropped.
# question Dunkelblau: the examples use it as a colour noun (das Dunkelblau). I merged it to the adjective because the lemma is overwhelmingly written lower-case.
# question Einchecke: the target could be the verb einchecken or the nominalised noun Einchecken; it is merged to the verb
# question Ergänzende: ergänzend may count as an adjective with its own entry; merged to the adjective rather than to ergänzen.
# question Erlernte: Nominalised participle with only "erlernte(n)" attested; I kept it as a noun like "das Gute", but "merge:erlernen" is possible if the list should not hold nominalised participles.
# question Fedora: "der Fedora" (the hat) is a common noun, but the counted use is mostly software. I dropped it.
# question Finanzier: forms are mostly the verb finanzieren, but Finanzier is a valid noun, so kept; the verb forms may belong under finanzieren
# question Flint: lemma mixes the English word/name with Flinte (142 tokens); dropped rather than merged
# question Forsch: forms are mostly the verb forschen but also the adjective forsch; chose forschen as the majority
# question Fried: the forms are mostly the surname, but friede/friedes suggest some belong to Friede; dropped as a name
# question Fujifilm: a famous company brand might count as a "famous institution", but the rules only name institutions; I treated it as an ordinary name.
# question Fuldaer: a place-derived adjective is not a place name itself; dropped because it is tied to Fulda, which is not on the list.
# question Fünfte: The dictionary form of an ordinal (fünft vs fünfte) is not settled by the rules. I used fünft.
# question Gemahl: gemahlen is mostly a form of mahlen and is mixed in; kept Gemahl.
# question Ger: no obvious target, so dropped rather than merged.
# question Ginger: dropped as non-established English and a name in examples (Ginger Baker); Ginger Beer might count as a loan.
# question Giropay: it is a brand name, but a payment method learners meet in German shops, and the rules don't say whether such product names count as established loans
# question Glucose: it could be treated as a variant of Glukose (merge); kept because it is an accepted German spelling.
# question Gnome: all examples are the GNOME desktop software, not the plural of Gnom, so a drop as a name is also plausible.
# question Gunstgewerblerin: the text is spam-generated, so the merge into Kunstgewerblerin may not reflect real usage.
# question Haager: a demonym-style adjective from Den Haag (Haager Konvention); the rules cover place names but not derived adjectives, so I dropped it as place-derived.
# question Habsburger: dynasty name versus demonym noun; I treated it as a common noun.
# question Hambacher: the place-derived adjective (Hambacher Forst) is treated as a place name and dropped; the rules do not say whether such adjectives follow place names.
# question Hauf: the evidence is mostly "Haufe Akademie" (a name), so merging into Haufen is doubtful; drop would also be defensible.
# question Heyne: Heyne Verlag is a well-known publisher, but it is not clearly a "famous institution", so I dropped it
# question Hg: abbreviation could stand for Herausgeber (merge) or be a symbol/acronym (drop); chose drop
# question Hockenheimring: it is a famous racetrack, but the rules do not say whether that counts as a famous institution; I dropped it as a place name.
# question IDs: unclear whether ID is a listed word or a specialist acronym; I merged it to ID.
# question Ihrem: the lemma could be the capitalised polite form Ihr or lower-case ihr; I chose ihr
# question Illuminat: nearly all uses are plural (Illuminaten) for a historical group; kept as the singular lemma.
# question Indigen: the lemma is cut off from Indigene (noun, "die Indigenen"); I merged it, but keeping it as its own noun would also be defensible.
# question Innehalte: The examples are nominalised uses (das Innehalten), so Innehalten could be a noun in its own right. I treated the lemma as a cut-off fragment and merged it into innehalten.
# question Integrierte: all forms are "integrierten", so it could merge to integrieren (verb) or integriert (adjective); I chose the adjective because it is an attributive form.
# question Irgendwas: pronouns are not named in the case-merge rule; I applied the adjective/adverb/verb ruling by analogy.
# question Jage: the capitalised nominal infinitive "das Jagen" could count as a noun in its own right; merged because the only written form is lower-case "jagen"
# question Jumbo: examples mix the loan "Jumbo" with names and product uses, so I kept it as an established loan.
# question Klamm: counted text is only lower-case klamm (adjective?), kept as the noun Klamm, which may be wrong
# question Knacken: could be a noun in its own right (das Knacken, "crackling"), but the examples are verbal, so I merged it.
# question Kolosseum: counted as a famous landmark (like an institution) and a common noun, not as an unlisted place name.
# question Konstanzer: demonyms are not place names themselves; I treated them as place-derived and dropped them (same for Leverkusener, Ingolstädter). Confirm they are not meant to be kept as learnable adjectives or nouns.
# question Kreuzberger: this is a derived place-name adjective; the rules cover place names but not adjectives derived from them, so I dropped it.
# question Kürzere: it occurs only in the idiom "den Kürzeren ziehen". I merged to kurz, but keeping it as a fixed expression is also defensible.
# question Lei: counted forms mix Lei (currency or garland) and Leier (lyre); it is unclear which lemma applies, so I dropped it.
# question Limo: it is a clipped form of Limousine, and I could not tell whether Limousine is on the list; I kept it as an established short form.
# question Ling: the forms (ling, lingen, linge) are a mix of a suffix fragment, the place Lingen and names, so there is no obvious merge target; I dropped it.
# question Ländle: a dialect nickname for a region rather than a strict place name; I applied the place-name drop rule.
# question MWSt: I assumed Mehrwertsteuer is on the list; if it isn't, drop is the alternative.
# question Maggi: it is a brand name, but a very common one in German; I dropped it under the "other names drop" rule.
# question Marienkirche: a generic church name shared by many churches, not a single famous institution; kept as a common compound
# question Marktstraße: it is a compound common noun, but the evidence shows it used almost only as a street name, so I dropped it.
# question Mayo: is a clipping like this a lemma in its own right, or should it merge into Mayonnaise? I kept it as an established everyday word.
# question Mic: no examples; I assumed a clipping of Mikrofon, but it could be the English word "mic".
# question Mur: Mure (mudflow) is a real noun, but the lemma and written forms point to the river name.
# question Münsteraner: the demonym derives from a place name; the rules only say place names drop, so it is kept like Berliner
# question Nasch: examples are the nominalised Naschen; chose the noun rather than the verb naschen
# question Niedersachse: The lemma is a real demonym, but the counted forms are mostly the place name Niedersachsen. I dropped it under the place-name rule.
# question Niese: the examples show nominalised Niesen (noun) rather than the verb. I merged into the verb niesen as the clearly broken lemma. Should it be merge:Niesen instead?
# question Nordschleife: could count as a famous institution (Nürburgring) rather than a place name; dropped as a place name
# question Offizielle: the examples show adjective use, so I merged it. The noun der/die Offizielle is possible but rare.
# question Op: it could also stand for Operationssaal; merged to Operation.
# question Parallel: the written forms are mostly the brand "Parallels", so the lemma may be junk rather than a plural of Parallele; I merged it to Parallele.
# question Pdf: PDF may count as a specialist acronym (drop) rather than an established abbreviation; I merged it into PDF.
# question Poly: fragment of Polyester, Polytechnikum or something else is not obvious; dropped
# question Pranayama: it could count as an established loan in yoga vocabulary, but I treated it as a specialist foreign word.
# question Prototyping: English word, but kept as an established loan in technical German; this is borderline.
# question Pädophil: the examples show nominalised forms (Pädophiler), which could count as a noun in its own right. I merged it because the counted forms are lower-case adjectives.
# question Raptor: it is a brand name here, but could also pass as a loan word (a type of dinosaur).
# question Rech: the data shows "rechen" (195) and "rech" (151); I assumed the noun Rechen (rake), but the verb rechen is possible.
# question Reel: unclear whether "Reel" (Instagram/video) counts as an established loan; dropped as not yet established.
# question Rheinmetall: whether a major company counts as a "famous institution" is unclear. I dropped it.
# question Rit: Riten is the plural of Ritus, but rit and rites do not fit that lemma. I merged into Ritus anyway.
# question Ruhrpott: It is a colloquial region name, and I treated it as a place name to drop.
# question Rück: could be a prefix of Rück-words, but no single target is obvious; dropped
# question Sach: forms sach/sachs may be the name Sachs rather than a fragment of Sache; merged to Sache
# question Sachse: the surface form "sachsen" (141) is probably mostly the place name Sachsen, but the lemma is a valid noun, so kept
# question Schütte: the written forms are lower-case verb forms, but Schütte is also a noun (chute) and a surname; I treated it as the verb.
# question Sechste: could be the nominalised "der/die Sechste", but the written forms are mostly the ordinal adjective, so I merged to the adjective sechste.
# question Sechster: it could be a nominalised person (the sixth-placed finisher) that stays as its own noun; I treated it as an inflected ordinal and merged it to sechste.
# question Segway: genericised brand; kept as an established loan, but the rules do not say whether brand-derived common nouns count
# question Selbstständiger: it is a nominalised adjective in masculine form, and the rules cover "das Gute" and gendered pairs but not this exact case; I treated it like Lehrerin.
# question Sexcam: it is a compound loan seen mostly in spam contexts; it is not clear whether it counts as an established loan
# question Sextreffe: spam-like form; the rules give no drop reason for spam, but I dropped it as junk rather than merging into Sextreffen.
# question Shemal: the rules have no category for offensive or spam terms; I dropped it
# question Slideshow: unclear whether it is an established loan or just an English word (kept as a common German usage)
# question Soap: examples show the cosmetics brand "Soap & Glory"; kept as an established loan, but the frequency may be inflated by the brand
# question Streu: the forms streu/streuen may mostly be the verb streuen rather than the noun; I kept it as a real noun.
# question Stör: could be the fish noun Stör, but the counted forms are mostly stören.
# question Syriza: a political party might count as a famous institution; dropped like Rammstein.
# question Te: a fragment with no obvious target word, so the rule to merge fragments can't be applied; I dropped it.
# question Terminator: also a generic noun (plural Terminatoren); dropped as a film title/character
# question Thal: "Thal" may also be a place or surname; I merged it to Tal as an archaic spelling.
# question Thieme: famous medical publisher might count as a famous institution; dropped as a name
# question Thumbnail: borderline established loan; kept because it is common in German tech texts.
# question Todesser: This is an established German term for a fictional group, not a person's name. The rules do not cover fictional terms, so I dropped it as a name-like word.
# question Twister: the game/brand name is borderline between a loan and a proper noun; kept as an established loan.
# question Tüv: lemma is spelled in lower case; the rules call for keeping famous institutions, but a merge to TÜV may be better.
# question UA: could be merged into a lemma for "unter anderem" if one is listed; none is assumed, so drop.
# question Ultras: could be a plural-only noun with its own meaning (football fans); I merged it into the singular Ultra.
# question Unendliche: it may be the nominalised "das Unendliche" (a noun in its own right, keep), but the sample has no examples, so I merged it to the adjective.
# question Unilever: company name, not clearly a "famous institution"; dropped as an other name
# question Unimog: a brand name that is also a common noun (Unimog, Unimogs). I treated it as a name and dropped it.
# question Uranus: planet name not covered by the place-name rule; treated as other name.
# question Ver: it is a cut-off fragment (ver, vern), but no merge target is obvious, so I dropped it instead of merging.
# question Verf: Verfasser is assumed from the abbreviation; no examples were available to confirm.
# question Verkleben: examples are nominalised infinitives (des Verklebens); the rules don't say whether a nominalisation counts as a noun in its own right like Essen. I merged it.
# question Vermisste: it could be merged into the adjective "vermisst" or kept as a nominalised noun. I kept it because the rules leave nominalised adjectives as nouns.
# question Volkszeitung: It is a generic compound, but all the examples are regional newspaper names. I dropped it as a non-famous name.
# question Vot: the written forms are English "votes"; merged to Vote, but if Vote is not accepted as an adapted loan this should be drop.
# question Vs: "versus" may not be on the list; merge assumed, drop if absent.
# question WISSEN: the written form is lower-case wissen. I merged into the verb, but the noun Wissen is also plausible.
# question Weis: it could be a dropped ß (weiß), but the written forms and POS don't show whether that means wissen or white, so I dropped it.
# question Wenigste: nominalised superlative (das Wenigste). I merged it to wenig, but it could count as a noun in its own right.
# question Wett: fragment target unclear (Wette vs Wettbewerb), no examples
# question ZHAW: a Swiss university abbreviation; unclear whether it counts as a famous institution, so I dropped it.
# question Zollverein: the historical customs union is a common noun, but the examples are the Essen site, so dropped as a place name
# question ala: it is the loan "à la" written without its accent. It is unclear whether a two-word expression is a valid lemma target.
# question angaben: no examples; could be the noun Angaben, but tagged VERB and the screener says angeben, so chose angeben
# question angegriffen: only inflected adjective forms are counted; kept because the adjective has its own dictionary entry, but this is borderline.
# question anhin: No clear target exists, since "bis anhin" is a real Swiss idiom. I merged it into bisher as the closest standard equivalent.
# question ankleiden: the lemma is a real verb, but the examples are the noun Ankleide (dressing room), which would need its own lemma Ankleide.
# question aufgaben: could be the plural noun Aufgabe, but the tag is VERB and it is lower case, so I merged to aufgeben.
# question aufgegeben: unclear whether the participle has its own adjective entry; I treated it as a verb participle.
# question bauschen: bauschen is a real verb, but the counted forms are almost all the surname Bausch; dropped.
# question befassten: the rules don't say whether a pronominal verb's merge target carries "sich"; I used "sich befassen"
# question begünstigt: the forms are mostly nominalised, and it is unclear whether begünstigt has an adjective entry of its own.
# question beigeordnet: could instead be treated as the participle of beiordnen; merged to the noun Beigeordnete because the examples are mostly nominal
# question bes: forms beser/besste suggest a form of gut/besser, but the lemma is not obviously recoverable, so dropped
# question besucht: unclear whether "besucht" (as in "gut besucht") counts as an adjective with its own entry; I merged to besuchen.
# question beta: "Beta" could count as an established loan; here it appears as a code or English word, so I dropped it.
# question center: German "Center" exists as a loan, mostly in compounds; I merged it into Zentrum as an English spelling of an adapted word.
# question consulting: unclear whether Consulting is an established loan with its own entry; merged under the case rule
# question darunterliegen: the forms are mostly participles, but the lemma is the infinitive, so I kept it.
# question deal: the forms are a mix of "Deal" and "Dealer"; I merged to Deal, but Dealer could be its own lemma.
# question dent: "al dente" is an established loan phrase, but the rules give no multi-word lemma to merge it into, so I dropped the fragment.
# question dipl: it could abbreviate Diplom or diplomiert, so the target is not obvious; I dropped it rather than merging.
# question documenta: the written form is always lower-case and the official name is "documenta", so merging into a capitalised "Documenta" is possible, but I kept it as a famous institution.
# question drinne: rules don't cover dialect variants; chose merge:drinnen
# question drosera: Drosera is also the German name of the plant (Sonnentau), but it reads as a specialist Latin name, so dropped instead of merge:Drosera.
# question durchnässen: infinitive filed as lemma but nearly all uses are the adjective durchnässt; kept as a valid verb lemma, though merging into durchnässt could be argued.
# question eShop: could be merged into a Shop-type lemma, but brand-like spelling so dropped
# question eich: no examples; Eiche is assumed from the forms eiche/eichen, though "eicher" could also be a different word (e.g. Eicher as a name)
# question eingemacht: could be merge:einmachen as a participle, but the examples are the nominalised "das Eingemachte"; I chose merge:Eingemachte.
# question eingeschrieben: may count as an adjective with its own entry (eingeschriebener Brief), in which case keep.
# question einzuloggen: the examples are reflexive, but I merged to plain "einloggen" because the verb also works without "sich"
# question erscheinend: no examples; merged on the assumption it is a present participle with no adjective entry
# question ersteinmal: the correct form is the two-word erst einmal, but lemmas are single tokens, so I used erstmal
# question erstenmal: a multi-word phrase "erstes Mal" has no single lemma; merged to Mal as the closest option
# question flash: the forms (flashen, flasher) point to a verb, but the noun Flash is also possible; I chose flashen.
# question forte: it is a music term and a loan word, tagged as a verb; I kept it as an established loan, but it could count as a specialist term.
# question frites: it is only the second half of "Pommes frites"; I merged it into Pommes, but the multiword lemma may be the better target.
# question gefassten: could be merged to the adjective gefasst (calm) instead of fassen; examples show the participle of fassen
# question gefestigt: unclear whether it has its own adjective entry or is only the participle of festigen; I kept it on the verheiratet/bekannt analogy.
# question gehört: the forms could also belong to gehören, so hören is a guess
# question gelöst: the examples are mostly the participle of lösen, but the adjective gelöst has its own entry.
# question ger: only the fragment forms ger/gere/geren are given, with no example, so no target could be identified; dropped.
# question geschmiedet: may count as an adjective with its own entry (keep); I merged it into schmieden.
# question getrieben: it could count as an adjective with its own entry; the evidence shows compounds like "Vocal getriebenen", so I merged it into treiben.
# question gruppen: could be the plural of Gruppe or a form of gruppieren; I chose Gruppe, the likelier source.
# question grösst: the lemma is an inflected superlative in Swiss spelling, and merging to the positive "groß" is the closest dictionary form, but the rules don't say whether a superlative goes to the positive.
# question größen: tagged VERB with forms größen/größenen, so Größe is a guess; could also be a verb form of a different word.
# question haette: the real word hätte is itself a conjugated form of haben; I merged into the dictionary form haben rather than hätte.
# question heit: the cut-off suffix fragment (-heit) has no obvious target lemma, so it is dropped rather than merged
# question hintern: could be the noun Hintern or the colloquial contraction of "hinter den"; the single example suggests a noun
# question hlt: hält vs. hielt/hälten ambiguous, no examples; chose halten
# question hochstehen: the counted forms are all the adjective hochstehend; kept as the verb lemma, but merging into hochstehend is arguable.
# question hr: could also be hör-forms (hören), but ihr/ihrer/ihren fit the counts best.
# question htte: hätte is a form of haben, but there is no separate lemma for it; I merged into the infinitive.
# question individualisierte: unclear whether individualisiert has its own adjective entry like bekannt. I merged it to individualisieren.
# question int: the fragment has no examples and no obvious source, so I dropped it.
# question konstante: could also be the noun Konstante, but it is lower-case and tagged as an inflected adjective, so I chose merge:konstant
# question leerstehend: unclear whether the participle counts as an adjective with its own entry; I kept it.
# question lter: the dictionary form of älter is alt (comparative); merged there rather than to a comparative lemma.
# question mallorquinisch: unclear whether adjectives derived from place names are covered by the place-name drop rule; chose keep
# question manag: the surface forms include "managen" (51) as well as "manager" (412); I merged to Manager, but managen is also possible.
# question medium: the sample sentence uses it for steak doneness, which is closer to an adjective than to the noun Medium. I merged it to Medium.
# question metallic: it could be kept as an established loan or merged into a German adjective such as metallisch, and the rules don't settle which
# question mist: it includes mister (24), an English "Mr" use, which does not belong under Mist. I merged it to Mist.
# question nachhause: dictionary lists "nach Hause" as two words, so there is no single lemma to merge into; kept as an accepted variant
# question nachrichten: the lower-case form could merge to Nachricht or Nachrichten; chose Nachrichten because the plural has its own meaning (news).
# question nehmend: it is a participle fragment (as in zunehmend or abnehmend), and several parents are possible, so I dropped it.
# question neo: It is a prefix fragment with no obvious merge target, so I dropped it.
# question ni: forms (nies, nider, niere, niem) have no single obvious target, so dropped rather than merged.
# question nnen: it is probably a broken-encoding remnant of a verb ending (e.g. können), but the stem is unknowable, so I dropped it.
# question nude: its status as an established loan is borderline (it appears mainly in fashion contexts). I kept it.
# question paarmal: Duden accepts "paarmal", but the screeners called it misspelt (ein paar Mal). I kept it.
# question port: Port (harbour, noun) may be intended, but the forms look like English "port", so I dropped it.
# question rang: forms ranger/range/ranges look like English or noun Rang, not ringen; merged to ringen on the main form
# question rbb: Unclear whether a regional public broadcaster counts as a "famous institution" (keep) or a specialist acronym (drop); I dropped it as a lower-case abbreviation.
# question rechten: mixed lemma (adjective recht, verb rechten, noun Recht); chose merge:recht
# question recyceltem: the dictionary form could be the adjective "recycelt" or the verb "recyceln". I chose the verb.
# question reisst: could be reißt (reißen) or reist (reisen); I chose reißen.
# question sal: "Sale" is an English-origin loan; I merged it on the assumption that "Sale" is the listed adapted lemma. If it is not on the list, this should be a drop.
# question saturn: examples show a retailer/brand, but it could be the planet Saturn (which would be merge:Saturn); I dropped it as a name.
# question schellen: the tag is NOUN but the forms are mostly verb forms; I kept it as the verb lemma.
# question schlag: could be the noun Schlag/Schlager or the imperative of schlagen; I chose schlagen.
# question schränken: forms could belong to einschränken, beschränken or verschränken; no examples to tell, chose einschränken per screener
# question schränkt: the likely source is einschränken (separated prefix), but beschränken is also possible.
# question sechst: the dictionary form of the ordinal is unclear; I chose "sechste".
# question sen: cut-off fragment with no clear source word, so I dropped it instead of merging.
# question sep: It could be an abbreviation of September or of something else, and no examples were found.
# question shar: the forms are English "share"; merging into a lemma "share" would add an English word, so I chose drop.
# question spuren: the surface forms mix spuren (to obey) with spurt/spurten (sprint); I kept the lemma, but the counts may combine two verbs.
# question ss: the fragment could stand for many words (muss, dass, etc.), so there is no obvious merge target and I dropped it.
# question stick: written forms mix Stick, Sticker and English "sticks"; merged to Stick, though Sticker may deserve its own entry
# question store: could be the German loan Store (shop/curtain) but no examples to confirm
# question stv: it may abbreviate "stellvertretend", a listed word, which would make it a merge; I dropped it because the meaning is unclear.
# question stündig: cut-off fragment of -stündig compounds (einstündig, zweistündig…) with no single target lemma, so dropped rather than merged.
# question sähen: forms (sähen, gesäht, säht) fit säen, but the examples are the subjunctive of sehen; merged to sehen.
# question tantra: this may count as a specialist foreign word. I merged it into Tantra as a noun.
# question teilig: -teilig is a cut-off suffix with no obvious target lemma to merge into, so I dropped it.
# question ti: Fragment with no clear target. I merged it to Tier because tier accounts for 183 of the counted forms.
# question trau: the counted forms are trau (427) and trauer (34), so it could be trauen or Trauer; I chose trauen from the VERB tag
# question tägig: a cut-off fragment should be merged, but there is no standalone lemma to merge into (e.g. dreitägig); dropped
# question ua: abbreviation of "unter anderem" (a phrase), no clear lemma to merge into
# question ui: interjection with no example sentences and a wrong ADJ tag; kept as an established interjection, but it may be too informal for a learner list
# question ung: cut-off fragment with no clear source word (Ung-/-ung?), so I dropped it instead of merging.
# question unterbrochen: unclear whether it counts as an adjective with its own dictionary entry; I chose to merge.
# question ur: prefix fragment with no obvious target, so I dropped it.
# question uro: it may be a fragment of "euro", but the single example is a brand-like title, so I dropped it.
# question uvm: abbreviation of a multi-word phrase, with no single lemma to merge into; dropped
# question verkrachen: the forms are mainly the adjective verkracht (verkrachte Existenz); I kept the verb lemma.
# question verplanen: tagged ADJ with mostly "verplant" forms; I kept the verb infinitive as the lemma.
# question verstanden: the forms are all inflected, but verstanden could count as an adjective with its own entry; merged to verstehen
# question vhs: I assumed Volkshochschule is on the list; I did not check this.
# question vorneherein: the correct form is the particle in "von vornherein"; merged into vornherein, which may not exist as a lemma
# question weiterbildend: unclear whether it has its own adjective entry; chose merge to weiterbilden
# question widern: forms mix the preposition wider with the rare verb widern; merged to wider as the majority
# question wiedergeboren: could be filed as the participle of wiedergebären, but kept as an adjective in its own right
# question zuck: forms mix zuck, zucker and zucken, so the target is not obvious; chose merge:Zucker
# question Ärzteblatt: kept as a famous institution/publication, but it could also be read as an ordinary compound noun.
# question Bildende: does a participle in a fixed term ("bildende Kunst") count as an adjective with its own entry (keep bildend) or merge to the verb?
# question Symptomprüfer, Sextreffe: should compounds whose frequency comes only from template or spam text be dropped, even when well formed?
# question Weis, hr, eich: with no examples and several plausible targets (misspelt weiß/wissen vs surname; ihr vs hören vs Hr.; Eiche vs dialect "euch"), should these drop or merge to the majority reading?
# question angeklagt: forms mix the noun ("Angeklagter, warum…") with participle use ("angeklagter Arzt"); should mixed lemmas follow the NOUN tag or the majority use?
# question ersteinmal, ala: may merge targets be multi-word lemmas ("erst einmal", "à la"), and are they on the list? If not, should ersteinmal go to "erstmal"?
# question merkur: are planet and astronomical names (Merkur, Mars, Venus) learnable nouns or names to drop?
# question ssen, rang, bes: when there are no examples and the written forms point to several targets, should the lemma be dropped or held for a manual corpus lookup?
# question ti, zuck: if a fragment's own form is its largest share (ti 181, zuck 266), should it be merged into the commonest real word or dropped?
# question widern, Parallel: when most of a lemma's tokens belong elsewhere ("wider" as a preposition or a particle of widerspiegeln; the brand Parallels Desktop), should we merge on the minority real-word reading, as here, or drop?
# question wun, aufgaben, tank: written forms are case-folded and there are no examples, so noun versus adjective/verb readings (Wunde/wund, Aufgabe/aufgeben, Tank/tanken) were decided on frequency. Should ambiguous no-example cases instead be held for a corpus check?
# question Arbeiten: the forms are mostly "des Arbeitens" (nominalised verb), so I kept it rather than merging into Arbeit, but merge:arbeiten would also be defensible.
# question BMWi: a ministry acronym could count as a famous institution, but I dropped it as a specialist acronym; the listed BMWI probably needs the same decision.
# question Balle: assumes Ballen is on the list; if it isn't, it should still become Ballen.
# question Bellen: the genitive "bellens" hints at a name, but the verb bellen seems more likely; I merged into bellen.
# question Bewerbe: forms bewerben/bewerbes could be the verb or the Austrian noun Bewerb; I took the noun reading because of the NOUN tag and "bewerbes".
# question Bl: unclear what word it abbreviates (BL is on the list as PROPN); dropped as a fragment.
# question Blatter: the forms blattern/Blatter (smallpox) could count as an established plural-only noun; I dropped it as mostly the surname.
# question Blutgefäßen: the listed singular is Blutgefäße, which is itself a plural; I merged into Blutgefäß.
# question Buchs: the written forms are buchsen, which could also be the verb buchsen or the Swiss place Buchs; decided merge:Buchse.
# question Buse: the written forms are mostly busen (Busen), but I couldn't confirm that Busen is already on the list as a lemma.
# question Börde: it is also a common geographic noun (fertile loess plain), but every example is a place name, so I dropped it.
# question DVDs: I assumed the singular lemma is spelled DVD; confirm it is on the list.
# question Dell: Unclear whether this is a person or the company. Both are names, so dropped.
# question Deutsche: the lemma is a legitimate nominalised adjective (der/die Deutsche), but the counted forms are mostly lower-case adjective inflections; I merged into deutsch, though 'Deutsch' (capitalised) is the entry on the list
# question Erfurter: could be treated as a noun (inhabitant), but the examples are adjectival, so merged into erfurter.
# question Erziehungsberechtigte: the list also has 'Erziehungsberechtigt' as a lemma, which looks like the broken entry; I kept this one because it is a nominalised adjective.
# question Essener: demonym adjective of the city Essen; dropped as place-derived, though the rules don't mention demonyms
# question Etikette: Etikette (etiquette) is also a real word, but the examples all mean labels (Etikett).
# question Finke: some forms (finken) are plurals of Fink, but the examples are mostly names; I dropped it.
# question Finne: the word is a real noun (pimple, fin) and a place name; the examples show the weak form of Finn and a place use, so I merged it into Finn.
# question Fr: "fr" could be für or another word with a lost umlaut (fren suggests a verb such as führen/fühlen); I chose für as the most likely.
# question Französische: it may be the nominalised "das Französische", but the evidence is adjective inflection, so I merged it into französisch.
# question Freien: unclear whether the lemma should be "Freie" (im Freien) or merged elsewhere; I kept it as an established noun use.
# question Fußballspielen: kept as a nominalised infinitive, the same reasoning as Arbeiten, since the screener's singular Fußballspiel is a different word.
# question Gelsenkirchener: it is a toponym-derived demonym, both a noun ("der Gelsenkirchener") and an indeclinable adjective; I merged it into the listed lower-case entry, but it could be dropped as a place-name derivative.
# question Glätten: it could be a nominalised infinitive (a noun in its own right), but I merged it into the verb.
# question Grundig: it is a brand name and also a surname. The rules don't say whether a company brand counts as a "famous institution", so I dropped it.
# question Gräbern: the screener points at 'Gräber' as the singular, but that is itself a plural; I merged into Grab.
# question Größere: merged into groß, but the comparative größer might be the preferred dictionary lemma
# question Grüße: Grüße is almost always plural (greetings) and may count as a plural with its own meaning; I merged it into Gruß.
# question Hande: the form is unclear (garbled Hände vs. other); decided merge:Hand.
# question Harzer: it is a toponymic adjective or demonym of Harz (Harzer Käse), not a plural. The rules don't say whether such derived forms count as place names, so I dropped it.
# question Hmmm: this is an interjection tagged VERB, and hmmm may not be worth keeping as a lemma; I merged it only on the case rule.
# question Homosexuelle: the listed Homosexuell (885) looks like a mis-lemma of the same noun. I kept Homosexuelle as a nominalised adjective, but the two may need merging in the other direction.
# question Kreative: the form is lower-case "kreativen", but a nominalised "die Kreativen" could be a noun; merged into the adjective.
# question Lande: written form is only landen with no examples; I assumed the verb landen rather than a noun plural.
# question Lauren: could be a form of the verb lauern, but it is most likely the English given name; I dropped it.
# question Letzte: 'letzte' is itself an inflected form on the list; I merged to the dictionary form letzt, which may not exist as a lemma.
# question Mag: could be the title abbreviation 'Mag.' (Magister), which the rules would drop or merge as an abbreviation; the conjugated-verb reading is more frequent
# question Maniere: Manieren is a plural with its own meaning, so merged there rather than into Manier; check that Manieren exists as a lemma.
# question Mia: a case-pair merge would point at 'mia', which is itself a name, so I dropped it.
# question Munich: Dropped as a place name. If München is not on the reviewed list, a merge into it could be considered.
# question Nichten: the real plural of Nichte exists, but the evidence is spam text using "nichtens" for "nicht". I dropped it; if Nichte should be taught, it needs a separate entry.
# question Novell: the company is the main sense, but the adjective novell is also on the list; dropped as a company name
# question Nächste: lower-case 'nächste' is tagged NOUN on the list while this one is ADJ; merged to the lower-case entry although the tags disagree
# question Passe: Passe is also a real noun (yoke of a garment), but every attested form is passen, so I treated it as the verb.
# question Pellets: Pellets is nearly always plural in use, so it might count as plural-only and be kept; I merged it into Pellet because the singular is on the list.
# question Petite: it is a French adjective in spam context; I dropped it rather than merging into the listed "Petit".
# question Plane: written lower-case plane, which could be a verb form of planen; I kept it as the noun Plane.
# question Playliste: Playlist and Playliste are both used, and the rules don't say which one is the lemma; I merged into the listed Playlist.
# question Porter: could be the beer or an English surname/place name; no examples were available, so I kept it as a loan noun.
# question Quere: the suggested singular 'Quer' is an adverb/prefix, so I kept Quere as its own noun.
# question Rausche: rauschen could be the verb or the nominalised noun Rauschen; I merged it into the verb.
# question Reiche: written forms are mostly the verb 'reichen' and the plural of Reich; kept as the nominalised adjective 'der Reiche', but a merge into Reich is arguable
# question Reisen: could be the plural of Reise or the nominalised infinitive; most forms are "reisens", so I merged it into reisen.
# question Rote: the written forms are lower-case roten/rote, so I treated them as the adjective rot; if some are the nominalised noun (der Rote), a separate keep might apply.
# question Schaller: the armour term "Schaller" (helmet) exists but is rare, and the examples are mostly the surname; decided drop.
# question Schnitte: 'Schnitte' (slice) is itself a valid noun, but the evidence is plural of Schnitt, so I merged it.
# question Single: tagged ADJ with an ADJ pair 'single', but the forms (singles, Singles) look like the established noun; kept as the noun
# question Staune: merged into the verb staunen, but the noun Staunen is equally plausible; the tag is NOUN and the examples are missing.
# question Süsse: the list holds "Süss"; I merged to the standard-spelling adjective süß, but the Swiss-spelling lemma may need its own handling.
# question Testen: no examples are available, so I couldn't tell whether this is the nominalised noun or just the verb in the infinitive.
# question Therme: the 'Therm' lemma the screener cites looks broken, so Therme may be the real singular and Therm may need merging into it.
# question Tourer: dropped because it only occurs in model names; if Tourer counts as an established loan (Tourenwagen-style "Tourer"), it should be keep.
# question Treffe: written as treffen, which could be the verb or the noun Treffen (meeting), and no examples were found; I merged it into the verb.
# question Unterlagen: kept as a plural with its own meaning (documents), though singular Unterlage (pad, base) is also on the list
# question Verbinden: there are no examples to show whether it is the button-label verb or the noun "das Verbinden". The genitive "verbindens" hints at noun use, but I merged to the verb.
# question Verbund: the lemma is a capitalised noun tagged VERB, but the written forms are mostly "verbunden". I merged to verbunden (an adjective with its own entry) rather than to verbinden.
# question Verstellbare: "verstellbare" is itself on the list as an inflected form, so the dictionary form "verstellbar" may need its own check.
# question Vllt: both vllt and Vllt are abbreviations of vielleicht; I merged straight into vielleicht rather than into vllt.
# question Wissenschafter: could merge into Wissenschaftler if the list treats regional variants as the same lemma; decided keep.
# question Wusstest: the listed wusstest is itself a conjugated form and may need merging into wissen too.
# question Zoller: it mixes a surname with the colloquial "15,6 Zoller" (inch-sized device); I dropped it as mostly a name.
# question Zuger: whether Swiss demonyms with -er count as learnable nouns; decided drop.
# question aufMehr: dropped as a concatenation artifact; the case-pair AufMehr (frequency 51) is probably junk too and needs checking.
# question ch: the forms ch/che/chen/cher/ches look like leftovers from split tokens; no target lemma, so dropped
# question dasMehr: could be the brand "DasMehr" (PROPN, 96 on the list) or the noun "das Mehr"; I merged it to Mehr, which I could not confirm is on the list.
# question dürr: the screener flagged it as a case pair, but the lowercase adjective form is correct, so I kept it.
# question eBook: Duden spells it E-Book; unclear whether Ebook or eBook should be the canonical lemma, and I merged into the existing Ebook.
# question eau: 'Eau' (PROPN) is on the list; I dropped this one as a fragment of Eau de Toilette/Cologne rather than merging into a possibly junk entry.
# question erspart: the "Erspart" entry on the list looks broken itself; I merged into Ersparte as the intended nominalised adjective.
# question eugen: merging into the name Eugen would be consistent with the case rule, but that target is itself a name to drop.
# question ge: all forms look like cut-off pieces of ge- words; I dropped it because no target is obvious, and the listed 'GE' looks like an acronym.
# question gend: Merged to Gender on the assumption that Gender is a valid lemma. The existing "Gend" entry is also a fragment.
# question ggmbH: gGmbH is a specialist abbreviation (and tagged ADJ), so it may itself be a drop under the abbreviation rules; I followed the case-merge ruling and merged into the existing lemma.
# question gute: tagged NOUN and paired with 'Gute', but the written forms (guteer, guteste) are adjective inflections, so I chose merge:gut over merge:Gute.
# question hellen: Forms mix the adjective hell with the verb hellen (hellt, hellte); I chose hell as the dominant reading.
# question iPhon: Assumes iPhone is the correct lemma. Whether it is on the list was not checked.
# question interessanterweise: the lowercase form is the correct one, so the capitalised 'Interessanterweise' on the list is probably the one that is broken.
# question kHz: 'KHz' is also listed, so dropping kHz as a specialist unit abbreviation may leave KHz needing the same review.
# question kWh: A common everyday unit symbol. I dropped it as an abbreviation, but if it should be kept, the lowercase 'kwh' needs the same decision.
# question klamm: forms are mostly Klammer (1,415) but the lemma is klamm; merged into Klammer rather than Klamm or the adjective klamm, and Klammer may not itself be on the list
# question kw: I merged into kW on the strength of the case-pair ruling, though kW is itself a specialist abbreviation.
# question lübben: 'Lübben' is already on the list as PROPN, which conflicts with the rule that any place name still seen is not on the list; dropped as a place name
# question mA: milliampere, a specialist abbreviation; the listed 'ma' is not a clear target, so I dropped it.
# question mbH: dropped as an abbreviation, but 'mbh' is already on the list and a merge:mbh would also be defensible.
# question ms: Merging into MS (Multiple Sklerose) would change the meaning, so dropped as an abbreviation instead.
# question nichtsdestotrotz: The capitalised 'Nichtsdestotrotz' (NOUN, 82) on the list looks like it should merge into this lowercase adverb.
# question nächste: the dictionary form is nächst, but 'Nächste' is already on the list; I merged into nächst.
# question oz: merged into Oz, but Oz is itself an abbreviation or name and may need its own review.
# question pH-Wert: the pair 'ph-Wert' on the list is the wrongly cased one and probably should merge into this
# question q: merged into Q by the case rule, but a single letter may not be learnable at all.
# question rauen: The forms are mixed (the noun Raute, the adjective rau and the plural Rauen). Merged into the majority, Raute.
# question reimen: tagged NOUN with verb forms, while the noun 'Reimen' is also listed; kept as the verb lemma, but a merge into Reimen or Reim may be intended
# question samen: 967 of 2,003 occurrences are 'gesamt', and the rest are -same/-samen fragments. I merged into Samen by the case rule, but merge:gesamt is arguably better.
# question tz: the forms tzen and tzes suggest broken words with lost letters, and the target is not obvious, so I dropped it rather than merging into TZ.
# question umkleiden: the forms mix the noun Umkleide with the verb umkleiden; I chose the noun because of the NOUN tag.
# question vanessa: "Vanessa" is already on the list, but other names drop under the rules; I dropped this one rather than merging into a name.
# question vidya: Vidya is also on the list as a case pair, but it is a name/Sanskrit term and probably should leave the list too; decided drop for this entry.
# question walther: Walther is a personal name, and the rule says other names drop; I merged it only because Walther is already on the list.
# question wichtigste: could be merged to Wichtigste (noun) instead of wichtig; the examples are missing, so I treated it as the inflected superlative adjective.
# question zion: 'Zion' is also on the list, but the examples show a place name (Zion National Park), so I dropped it rather than merging.
# question zustandekommen: the verb forms are tagged NOUN; keep as the verb, or merge into the nominalisation Zustandekommen?
# question Ü: a single letter tagged VERB; merged into ü as a case pair, though it may be better dropped as a non-word.
# question Erfurter/Harzer/Zuger/Essener: confirm the demonym rule "keep only if the place is on the reviewed list" (is Erfurt on it? why is Essener dropped while Kölner is kept?)
# question Fahren/Spielen/Reisen/Langlaufen: should productive nominalised infinitives (das Fahren, das Reisen) merge into the verb, as decided here, or count as nouns in their own right like Essen? For Langlaufen, should the target be langlaufen (merged here, consistent with the others) or the existing noun Langlauf?
# question Grundig/LEGO: do well-known brands count as "famous institutions" (keep) or as "other names" (drop)?
# question Grüsse: should Swiss ss lemmas (Gruss, Süsse) fold into ß forms (Gruß, süß)? Grüße already goes to Gruß.
# question Langlaufen: should a nominalised infinitive stay `keep` when a near-synonymous noun (Langlauf) is already on the list, or merge into that noun? Same question for Skifahren and Schneeschuhwandern.
# question Oberst, rauen, Reiche: these lemmas mix two real words (colonel/top, Raute/rau, Reich/die Reichen). Can they be split, or does the majority reading decide?
# question Schreie/Wehre/Bewerbe/Heile: with no examples, forms like -en are ambiguous between a noun plural and a verb; I decided by the NOUN tag and the genitive -es forms; confirm this test
# question Testen, Grille: should pure verb nouns (das Testen, das Grillen) merge into the verb? Reviewer 1 cites Scannen as kept.
# question erspart/Dritten: is 'Ersparte' on the list as a target? Should capitalised 'Erspart' also merge into it? Should merge targets not yet on the list (drittens, Ersparte) be created?
# question kWh, kB, kHz, ms: the README says units are not candidates, but the rules say specialist abbreviations drop. I kept the correctly cased symbols. Please confirm, and confirm that kwh/KB/KHz merge into them.
# question klamm, gend: the lemmatiser files nouns that look like comparatives (Klammer, Gender) under a made-up base word. Merging klamm leaves the real adjective klamm (clammy) without an entry. Should it be added?
# question wippen/Verbund: these mixed lemmas are kept for their own reading, but the forms belonging to another word (wippe → Wippe, verbunden) can't be reassigned; should such forms be split?
# question ADSL: borderline between a consumer acronym (like WLAN) and a specialist one (like HTML); kept.
# question Aloha: it could count as an established interjection, but the examples show it as a brand and technical name, so I dropped it.
# question Amin: chemistry noun (Amin) and a personal name are mixed; kept as the common noun, but it is specialist vocabulary
# question Audiodat: the true lemma is plural Audiodaten, with no common singular, so merged there
# question BEIM: beim is a standard contraction, not a colloquial one, so ruling 8 does not clearly say whether it merges into bei or stays its own lemma; I merged the case variant to beim
# question BVK: examples suggest Bundesverdienstkreuz in one and a trade association in another; dropped as an abbreviation/minor body
# question Bambini: Italian plural used as a collective in German youth sports; kept as is rather than merged to Bambino.
# question Bauspare: Bausparen is a lexicalised noun or productive infinitive, so the target could instead be bausparen
# question Belvedere: could be read as a landmark name (Vienna) or the common noun; kept as common noun
# question Beraterinne: the rules do not say whether the feminine or the masculine headword should be used; I chose the feminine form Beraterin.
# question Berghaus: counts mix the common noun with the place Berghausen; decided by the lemma's own reading.
# question Bermuda: also the common noun for shorts (Bermudas), but examples mix both; dropped as a place name
# question Boarding: borderline established loan; kept as air-travel vocabulary
# question Broadcast: borderline established loan, mostly tech/media contexts
# question Candida: this could be treated as a technical biological name; I kept it as a common medical noun.
# question Cheats: gaming loan, plural of Cheat; dropped as not established enough, could be a keep
# question Chromebook: it could be read as a genericised product noun (ruling 5) or as a brand; I dropped it as a brand.
# question Commonwealth: the English-looking foreign flag conflicts with its established use as the name of the British Commonwealth; kept as a nationally known institution.
# question Corso: the examples are mostly Italian street names, but Corso is also a German common noun; kept on its own reading (ruling 13).
# question DKMS: counted as a nationally famous organisation, but it is not named in the rulings; confirm it belongs with NABU and Greenpeace.
# question Datenschutzgrund: only the plural/dative forms are attested, but the singular is a regular compound, so I kept it.
# question Dresden: assumed to be on the reviewed place list, since it is a major city, so I merged into it.
# question ESSEN: the city Essen may be on the reviewed place list; merged into the common noun.
# question EVG: could be the rail union (Eisenbahn- und Verkehrsgewerkschaft, arguably nationally famous) or a local energy company; both readings appear, so I dropped it.
# question EXPO: world-fair sense kept, but part of the counts may be event or brand names.
# question Edda: the medieval Edda is a cultural work, but the examples mostly show the first name; dropped
# question Egli: Swiss fish name (perch) is a common noun, but the tagger and most usage read it as a surname
# question Estragon: tagged PROPN but a common herb noun, kept as a mistagged name.
# question Even: lemma is English "even"/surname Even; merge:even is a poor fit, drop would be safer if no German word exists
# question Exploit: IT jargon with only 2.5 Zipf in German; dropped as non-established, could go the other way
# question FAS: no examples, so unclear whether it is the newspaper (Frankfurter Allgemeine Sonntagszeitung) or something else; dropped
# question Fahrzeugdat: Fahrzeugdaten is plural-only in the text; I merged to the plural form since Fahrzeugdatum is rare.
# question Fleischhauer: tagged PROPN, but the examples show the Austrian common noun for butcher alongside surnames; kept
# question Gateways: Gateway is not clearly an established loan in German; merged on the assumption that it is.
# question Geldverdien: merged into Geldverdienen as a lexicalised noun, but it could instead go to the verb phrase "Geld verdienen" (rule 9).
# question Grizzly: counts are mostly the club name (Grizzlys, Grizzly Adams), but the lemma's own reading is the bear, so I kept it under ruling 13.
# question Grün-weiß: filed as a noun, but the examples are substantivised "Grün-Weißen" for a sports club; merged into the adjective.
# question HANNOVER: Hannover is not in this batch; I assumed it is on the reviewed place list. If it is not, drop.
# question Habsburg: a dynasty name that is also place-like; the rules cover neither case, so I treated it as a name and dropped it.
# question Halbe: capitalised "Halbe" can also be the noun for a half-litre beer, but the counts here are lower-case halbe/halben, so I merged to halb.
# question Hausen: could be the verb hausen (counts are lowercased), but it is tagged PROPN and the examples show none; dropped as a place name.
# question Hiernach: a lowercase adverb "hiernach" exists; merged on that basis
# question Hokkaido: tagged PROPN, but the examples use it as a common noun (the pumpkin). I kept it. Is the island Hokkaido a place that would be added to the list?
# question IDF: also an SEO term (inverse document frequency); kept as the military body by analogy with Hamas.
# question ILO: UN agency, not obviously "nationally famous" like UNO or NATO; dropped.
# question INS: counts are mostly the contraction "ins" (in das), which belongs to another lemma (ruling 13); I judged the acronym reading.
# question IVF: medical acronym that laypeople do use, but I treated it as technical and dropped it
# question Immo: it could be read as a colloquial abbreviation of Immobilie (rule 12), but the evidence looks like spam/brand text, so I dropped it.
# question Inbox: borderline English loan in business German; dropped.
# question Jasminute: lemma is a broken form; its counts are all Jasmin, which is also a first name, so I merged it into the plant noun.
# question KHK: medical acronym (koronare Herzkrankheit); dropped as specialist, but rule 12 could cover it as an abbreviation used as a word
# question KMK: nationally known education body or ministry-like acronym; dropped by analogy with ruling 3
# question KSK: Kommando Spezialkräfte is well known, but the examples are Künstlersozialkasse and Kreissparkasse; I dropped it as ambiguous and minor.
# question KURIER: examples show the Austrian newspaper, but the word is also the common noun Kurier; kept as common noun
# question Kehren: tagged PROPN, but the counts are probably mostly the verb kehren and the plural of Kehre; merged to the verb.
# question Keyframe: a specialist loan with fairly low diversity; I kept it as an established loan.
# question Knigge: mostly a surname in the examples, but also the common noun "der Knigge"; kept
# question Koch-Instituts: RKI is nationally famous, but research institutes are dropped under ruling 3, so I dropped it.
# question Landgraf: historical noble title (common noun) versus the surname seen in the examples; I dropped it under the "titles" rule.
# question Lofot: merge target Lofoten is an island group and may not be on the learnable places list.
# question Lycra: genericised brand per ruling 5 or plain brand; kept as common noun for fabric
# question MHD: abbreviation (Mindesthaltbarkeitsdatum); ruling 12 keeps abbreviations used as words, but the abbreviation exclusion could also apply
# question Malerarbeite: Malerarbeiten is mostly used in the plural, so it could stay as a plural-only keep; I merged it into the singular Malerarbeit.
# question Mastering: it could count as an established loan; I dropped it as specialist jargon.
# question Mekong: a major river, but rivers are not covered by the place list rules apart from the Themse. I dropped it. Should major foreign rivers be added?
# question Mignon: both a battery-size common noun and a name; kept as the common noun.
# question Ming: could be the Ming dynasty, but it reads as a name, so dropped.
# question NASDAQ: could count as a famous institution in finance texts, but I treated it as a company or exchange name
# question Neufundland: the island is a well-known place but not on the list; dropped, though Neufundländer (dog breed) is a real word
# question OUT: "out" is used in colloquial German ("out sein"), but it is mostly English text here; dropped
# question Ohrenschmerz: the form is almost always plural; merged into the plural-only headword Ohrenschmerzen, though Ohrenschmerz is also valid.
# question Ost-Berlin: not an explicit listed place; kept as a well-known historical city part.
# question PISA: it is a study, not an institution, and also a city name; I kept it as a well-known name.
# question POP: counts are uppercase in the tag but written "pop", so I merged it into Pop rather than treating it as an acronym.
# question Paleo: the diet name is common in German food writing but tagged as a name; dropped as name-like.
# question Panini: counts mix the sandwich with sticker-brand and museum uses; judged by the common-noun reading
# question Pompeji: famous ancient city, but not on the reviewed place list, so dropped under the "any place name you still see" rule
# question Protokolldat: Protokolldaten is effectively plural; the singular Protokolldatum is rare, so merged to the plural
# question Quebec: a major region of Canada, but not on the listed places; dropped.
# question RNA: technical acronym vs. school-level science word; kept as an ordinary word
# question Reunion: borderline loan for band reunions; dropped as not established enough
# question Rolli: could also be a name/nickname; kept as common noun
# question SMV: Schülermitverwaltung is a real school term, but the rules do not say whether such school-organisation acronyms count as words (like Kita) or as minor organisations; dropped as a minor organisation.
# question STOP: Stop and Stopp are both used in German, and I chose the Duden spelling as the target.
# question SWS: Semesterwochenstunden is a common academic abbreviation, but I treated it as niche and dropped it; rule 12 might apply
# question Santiago de Chile: a major capital, but it is in the batch, so I treated it as not on the reviewed list.
# question Sardinie: I assumed Sardinien is on the reviewed place list; if not, drop.
# question Scene: mostly English "Scenes" (132 of 320), but the German spelling variant is merged into Szene
# question Schwarzweiß: could be merged to adjective schwarzweiß; kept as the nominal use (in Schwarzweiß)
# question Scoring: established enough as a loan, or a specialist term to drop?
# question Shit: widely used colloquially in German, but I treated it as not established enough
# question Ska: tagged as a proper noun, but it is a music genre; I kept it as a common noun.
# question Sneakers: tagged PROPN, but the text uses it as a common noun, so I merged it to the singular Sneaker.
# question Sportschau: a named TV programme is not clearly covered by the institution list; kept as nationally famous (ARD).
# question Summa: "Summa summarum" is a German phrase, but the lemma is otherwise Latin or dialect for Sommer; dropped.
# question Südbade: lemma is a cut-off form of Südbaden, which is a region not on the listed places; dropped rather than merged
# question TAZ: the rules list "taz" in lower case, and the lemma is filed as TAZ; I kept it as the same newspaper.
# question Tadschikistan: a country, but it reached this batch, so per the rule that any place name still seen is not on the list I dropped it; check whether it was missed from the reviewed list.
# question Technische Universität Berlin: major university name, could count as nationally famous institution; dropped as a single university
# question Turkmenistan: a country, but the rules say any place name still in the batch is not on the list, so I dropped it.
# question UBA: Umweltbundesamt is a federal agency, not covered by the institution list; dropped like the federal-ministry acronyms
# question VHB: ad-style abbreviation; treated as excluded abbreviation rather than a word like Kita
# question Vans: could be the brand Vans, but examples show the plural of Van, so merged.
# question Verbindungsdat: the rules say to merge plurals to the singular (Verbindungsdatum), but the word is effectively plural-only, so I merged to Verbindungsdaten
# question Vermögensverhältnis: kept as the singular headword even though almost only the plural is attested.
# question WEF: not on the named-institutions list; kept as a famous organisation, drop if only German bodies count
# question Wahl-O-Mat: it is a product name of the Bundeszentrale für politische Bildung, but it is a nationally known word that German speakers use as a common noun, so I kept it.
# question Wannsee: famous via the Wannsee-Konferenz, but treated as a district name and dropped.
# question Waterloo: a place name by the rules, but also used as a common noun ("sein Waterloo erleben"); dropped as an unlisted place.
# question Weidmann: also common noun (hunter, "Weidmanns Heil"), but counts here are almost all the surname; dropped by the lemma's name reading
# question WikiLeaks: well known, but not listed among the nationally famous institutions, so dropped
