# question Wild-Symbol: a real compound, but it appears to be casino-slot jargon; I dropped it as spammy.
# question Windows: heavily used brand that is nearly a common noun; dropped as a brand
# question ZOOM: brand (video platform) in all examples, but also a common noun; kept as a common noun
# question angetraut: it is a participle that mostly appears as a noun-like adjective (angetraute Frau); I kept it as an adjective with its own entry.
# question br: could be the Bayerischer Rundfunk (a famous institution, keep), but the lowercase form and the 0.7% rate suggest the HTML tag; dropped.
# question burgenländisch: Burgenland is not named on the learnable places list; kept as a regional adjective, drop if the list excludes it
# question different: German does have the adjective differente, but the counts are mostly English; dropped.
# question maßgeben: the target maßgebend is chosen as the adjective, but the verb maßgeben is also possible
# question niederbayerisch: Niederbayern is a region, but not on the learnable list; dropped under the rule for place adjectives of unlisted places.
# question schwänz: could be the verb schwänzen or the plural of Schwanz (vulgar); chose the verb
# question these: counts are mostly English "these", so the lemma's own reading was used and it was merged into the German noun These
# question tollen: counts are mostly toll/till, so the lemma is judged by its own reading as the verb tollen
# question Äusserunge: the lemma is cut-off and Swiss-spelled; I merged to Äußerung, which combines the clipped-lemma and Swiss-ß rules.
# question ÖAMTC: not named in the rules, kept by analogy with the listed AOK, TK, DAK and Barmer
# question ÖV: Swiss-specific abbreviation (öffentlicher Verkehr); kept under ruling 12, but the shape rule excludes abbreviations.
# question Österreich-Ungarn: a historical state is not covered by the place rules; dropped as not listed.
# question Ötzi: a famous nickname but a person/figure; dropped under the "people" rule, so confirm no exception for widely known cultural figures.
# question Bermuda, Corso: should the garment (Bermuda, Bermudas) and the variant spelling Corso of Korso be merged into common-noun lemmas (Bermudashorts, Korso), even though the lemma's own reading is a place or a street?
# question Bork, pill: rule 13 was applied, so a lemma whose own spelling is a name or an English word was dropped and not merged into the German word that makes up most of its counts (Borke, Pille). Please confirm that drop is right rather than merge.
# question KMK, WEF: ruling 3 drops federal-ministry acronyms, but KMK (a conference of state ministers) and WEF (an international forum) are prominent in the news; keep them like UEFA and Greenpeace, or drop them?
# question ABS: Antiblockiersystem would be a word-like abbreviation (ruling 12) but counts are only lowercase abs, so dropped as ambiguous
# question Ag: counts are split between Ag (chemical symbol) and AG (Aktiengesellschaft, a common word); I decided by the lemma's own reading, but a separate AG lemma may need checking.
# question Albaner: the rules don't say whether Albanien is on the learnable list. I kept it as a nationality noun with its own feminine entry, but the Albaner See example is a place adjective.
# question B: z. B. is a multi-word abbreviation, so the rules on abbreviations used as words don't clearly cover a lone letter; dropped.
# question BLZ: it is an abbreviation like Kita or Lkw, but it is a form-field term, not clearly a word in general use; kept on that basis.
# question BWL: the earlier decision was drop, but ruling 12 covers abbreviations used as words, so keep.
# question BZgA: it is a federal agency, not a ministry, a party or a research institute, so no ruling covers it; I dropped it as a specialist acronym.
# question Basler: the rules do not say whether Basel is on the learnable list; assumed not, so dropped.
# question Berner: Bern is a capital but not on the learnable list, so dropped; check if Bern should be added
# question Blau-Weiß: in the examples it is mostly a club-name element, so it may be a name rather than a nominalised colour; I kept it per the earlier decision
# question Bundesverkehrsministerium: ruling 3 only covers acronyms such as BMZ. I kept the spelled-out compound, but it is a federal ministry and could be read as a drop.
# question CV: English abbreviation commonly used as a word in German job contexts; kept under ruling 12, though it could count as a non-established foreign abbreviation.
# question DM: abbreviation of a phrase (Deutsche Mark), also the dm brand; I kept it as an established currency abbreviation.
# question Dr: ruling 12 (abbreviations used as words keep) could also apply, but the rules on broken lemmas favour merging into Doktor.
# question EFH: it may count as an abbreviation used as a word (like WG or Lkw, which are kept), but it is a niche real-estate abbreviation; I merged it into Einfamilienhaus, assuming that word is on the list
# question Feinste: only occurs in the fixed phrase "vom Feinsten"; I treated it as a nominalised superlative and merged it into fein rather than keeping it as a lexicalised noun like das Gute.
# question Fewo: abbreviation of Ferienwohnung; kept under ruling 12, but the merge rule for abbreviations of listed words could apply
# question Frankfurter: Frankfurt is not named as a listed place, so I dropped it as a demonym; the sausage reading (a noun in its own right) would be keep if Frankfurt is on the list.
# question Frauenkirche: counts mix the Dresden landmark with the town Frauenkirchen (37 of 469); I decided by the lemma's own reading (landmark) under rulings 4 and 13.
# question Friese: Friesland is a region, not a listed place; kept as a person noun, but the place-derived rule could argue for dropping it.
# question Füs: the written forms "fuses" and "fus" look like a clipped or cut-off Fuß/Füße, but could also be foreign (English "fuses") or the abbreviation FüS; merged to Fuß following the earlier agreed decision.
# question GF: the rules don't say whether it counts as an established abbreviation like WG or Kita (Geschäftsführer). I dropped it.
# question Grass: this could be a Swiss-style or English spelling of Gras, but with no examples I dropped it as a likely surname.
# question Handeln: could be treated as lexicalised (das Handeln = conduct), but I treated it as a productive infinitive; the counts are only genitive handelns.
# question Hbf: Hauptbahnhof is assumed to be on the list. If it is not, the entry should be dropped.
# question Hesse: counts are mostly the surname (Hermann Hesse); kept by the lemma's own reading as the demonym under ruling 13.
# question JVA: could count as an abbreviation used as a word (ruling 12) or as an institution acronym; I kept it.
# question Kassier: counts include "kassieren" (2), a different word; decided on the noun reading.
# question Lesen: could also count as lexicalised (das Lesen as an activity); I treated it as productive and merged.
# question Linke: the counts are mostly the adjective link; I kept it on the noun/party reading.
# question MFG: not clearly covered by ruling 12 (written sign-off, not spoken); dropped.
# question MfG: it is an abbreviation of a phrase rather than a single word, so I dropped it instead of applying ruling 12.
# question Mi: same as Sa; "mi" may be mostly Spanish or a musical note rather than Mittwoch.
# question Mo: same as Sa; the "mosen" form suggests mixed readings.
# question Moskauer: I assumed Moskau is not on the learnable place list, so I dropped the demonym; if it is listed, this should be keep.
# question NA: counts are "na" (346) and "nas" (8); I read it as the interjection "na", so merged to lower-case na, which may not exist as a separate lemma.
# question Neo: counts are split between "neo" (986, a tech product term) and "Neos" (255, the Austrian party); I decided by the lemma's own reading and dropped it, which loses the party sense that ruling 3 would keep.
# question Neue: counts are mostly adjective forms (neuen, neue), so the lemmatiser may be mis-filing neu; decided by the lemma's own nominalised reading (ruling 13).
# question Nov: I assumed an abbreviation of a listed word merges to it (November) rather than being dropped as an abbreviation.
# question Nürnberger: earlier keep reversed because Nürnberg is not on the learnable list; also a sausage/insurer name
# question PDS: a German party, not one of the Swiss/Austrian parties the ruling names; kept as an established institution, but it could be treated as a minor party and dropped.
# question PLZ: could instead merge:Postleitzahl as abbreviation of a listed word; kept under ruling 12
# question PS: Postskriptum is an abbreviation used as a word (keep), but PS as a horsepower unit would be a unit symbol (drop).
# question Pflegebedürftiger: the -e and -er forms are separate lemmas of the same word; I kept the -e form and merged the -er form into it, following the Angestellte convention.
# question Pfälzer: Pfalz is a region, not a listed place, but Pfälzer is also a common noun (a person or a wine); I treated it as a demonym and dropped it.
# question Pl: it is unclear whether "Pl" is a listed abbreviation of Plural or just a stray abbreviation. I merged it, but it could also be a drop.
# question Pr: the lemma may also reflect "pres" (2 counts) and other readings; I took it as PR.
# question Prof: ruling 12 (abbreviations used as words) could also support keep; I chose merge:Professor because it is a clipping of a listed word.
# question Ps: unclear whether PS (Pferdestärke) counts as a unit symbol (drop) or an abbreviation used as a word like Lkw (keep); decided drop, as the lemma is a cased variant that also covers postscript.
# question RTW: it abbreviates Rettungswagen, but I don't know whether that word is on the list, so I dropped it instead of merging.
# question Rev: no example sentence available; could be an abbreviation of Reverend or Revision, dropped as unresolvable.
# question Richtige: counts are mostly lower-case richtige (adjective); I decided by the lemma's own noun reading (ruling 13), same for Wesentliche, Besondere, Wissenswerte, Übrige. Listed for the lemmatiser.
# question SZ: the rules do not name newspapers; I treated it as a famous institution, but it could count as a mere abbreviation.
# question Sa: day abbreviations are merged as abbreviations of listed words, but the counts may include other readings (SA, sa); the rules do not settle this.
# question Schlimmste: das Schlimmste could count as a lexicalised nominalisation like das Gute (keep); merged because the lemma is a superlative inflection.
# question Schwabe: unclear whether Schwaben is on the learnable list; kept as a common-noun demonym, though the examples are mostly surnames
# question SuS: nearly all counts are the Spanish word "sus", so the decision rests on the lemma's own German reading (ruling 13). Dropping it is defensible if the abbreviation is not wanted as a word.
# question Tempelhofer: Tempelhofer Feld is a Berlin landmark but not a German-named famous landmark like Eiffelturm; the adjective is dropped as a non-listed place derivative.
# question US: a common prefix (US-Präsident), but it abbreviates a place name; I dropped it. Merge:USA if USA is listed.
# question UVP: unclear whether it is an abbreviation used as a word (ruling 12) or a specialist acronym; I chose drop.
# question Verborgene: only occurs as "im Verborgenen". It could be read as a lexicalised noun (keep), but I merged it into the adjective.
# question Wirken: all the counts are the genitive form wirkens, and it is unclear whether das Wirken is lexicalised or a productive infinitive; I kept it.
# question Wohnen: could also count as lexicalised (das Wohnen as a housing topic); I treated it as productive and merged.
# question ag: written as ag/ages/age/ager/agem, which are mostly other-language forms. I read the lemma as the common German abbreviation AG (ruling 12) and merged it into AG. Please check that AG exists as a lemma; otherwise drop.
# question ca: no listed full word (circa) is shown; I kept it as an everyday abbreviation under ruling 12. Merge to the full word if it is listed.
# question einunddreißigste: the earlier "ordinals 1–30 only" ruling is not in the rules shown; I followed ruling 6 and kept it. If that limit is binding, drop it.
# question fr: could also be a cut-off fragment of another word, but "für" is by far the most likely.
# question gg: the abbreviation is ambiguous (gegebenenfalls vs. chat "good game"); I dropped it rather than merging it to a lemma that may not be on the list.
# question grüßen: the counts are mostly the noun Grüße (a plural of Gruß); I decided by the verb reading under ruling 13.
# question ichs: contraction of ich + es, so the host word is unclear; merged into ich, but es is also plausible. Tagged VERB.
# question lol: ruling 12 keeps abbreviations used as words, but I treated lol as an internet interjection rather than an established word and dropped it.
# question obs: it could be read as a fragment (the earlier drop), but I treated it as the ob's contraction under ruling 8. Please check that the counts are not mostly something else.
# question sry: could be merge:sorry as an abbreviation of a listed word, but I'm unsure "sorry" is on the list, so I dropped it as chat slang.
# question zB: abbreviation of a multi-word phrase, so it is neither a lemma to merge into nor clearly a word-like abbreviation; I kept it under ruling 12.
# question zweitgroß: the lemma is a stem built from an ordinal plus a superlative, so no dictionary form exists. I kept it as the family lemma.
# question Übrige: the only form counted is "übrigen", mostly in the fixed phrase "im Übrigen"; merge:übrig is possible if that phrase is treated as adjectival.
# question Allgemeine, Ähnliche, Schlimmste, Feinste, Jüngste, Verborgene: where is the line between lexicalised nominalised adjectives or superlatives (keep) and productive ones (merge into the adjective)? Ruling 9 only covers -en infinitives and das Gute.
# question Oct: should English month abbreviations in date stamps (Oct, Dec, May …) drop as foreign text, or merge into the German month as "English spellings of an adapted word"?
# question etc, usw, zB, ca: should written-only abbreviations be merged into their full word when it is a single lemma (ca -> circa) and dropped otherwise? Or does ruling 12 cover them?
# question Allerbeste: counts are mostly lower-case adjective forms (allerbeste/allerbesten), but the examples are the nominalised "das Allerbeste"; kept by analogy with das Gute.
# question Anlaß: counts are mostly anlassen forms, but the lemma's own reading is the old spelling of Anlass, so I merged it there.
# question Aphrodite: the rules don't mention mythological names; I treated them as "other names" and dropped, though Greek gods may be learnable like planets.
# question Aspe: the counts are nearly all "Aspen" (the place and the Aspen Institute), so the lemma can't be judged by its own reading. Aspe is a rare variant of Espe (aspen tree), so I dropped it; merging it into Espe would be an alternative.
# question Bab: the written forms (babes, bab, babs) look like English "babe"/a fragment, so no German target is clear; dropped.
# question Bento: it is a Japanese loan that is not clearly established in German; I kept it, but it could be dropped.
# question Bespiel: counts are mostly bespielen, but the lemma as written is a misspelling of Beispiel (example sentence), so I merged it into Beispiel under ruling 13
# question Dalton: it is also a unit (Dalton), but the examples are mostly surnames and the rules treat units as symbols, so I dropped it.
# question Delegierte: the headword form (Delegierte vs Delegierter) is not fixed by the rules; chose the -e form like Angestellte.
# question Einnahmen: the lemma is tagged VERB, but "einnahmen" is far more likely the plural noun than a conjugated form of einnehmen.
# question Eros: could also be the common noun Eros (love), but the counts are lower-case and read as a name, so I dropped it.
# question Fl: the abbreviation's expansion is unclear (Fläche, Florin?), so decided drop.
# question Freebie: Duden status as an established loan is uncertain; kept.
# question Freifunk: a community network name that is close to a common noun, but I treated it as a project name
# question Freiheitliche: it could be a lexicalised party-member noun (FPÖ); I treated it as an inflected adjective.
# question Gau: Counts may be dominated by the Nazi-era or regional administrative sense; I kept it as a real German noun.
# question Gesch: fragment could be Geschichte, Geschäft or geschehen (geschen), so no obvious host word; dropped
# question Gleichgesinnt: counts are mostly the nominalised "Gleichgesinnte" (people who share one's views); merged to the adjective, but it could be a lexicalised noun like Angestellte.
# question Grünliberale: nominalised adjective like Angestellte, but it names a minor party (GLP-type), so I dropped it.
# question Identitäre: could be merged into identitär, but kept as a lexicalised group noun like Angestellte.
# question Innerste: could be kept as a lexicalised noun (im Innersten), but I merged it to Innere.
# question Klapper: counts are mostly klappern (nominalised infinitive), but I kept the lemma by its own noun reading under ruling 13.
# question Kr: counts are mostly "Kren" (Meerrettich, Austrian/Bavarian), so the lemma is arguably a different word; dropped as an abbreviation or fragment.
# question Lastminute: English phrase written as one word; I kept it as an established travel-sector loan.
# question Liechtensteiner: I assumed Liechtenstein is on the learnable list; if it is not, this should be drop.
# question Litecoin: cryptocurrency names could count as established loans like Bitcoin; I treated this one as a specific brand.
# question Lk: could be Leistungskurs, Landkreis or the Gospel of Luke, with no clear single host, so I dropped it.
# question MO: I assumed Montag is on the list; if not, this should be dropped.
# question Poste: tagged ADJ with no examples; could be a plural of Post or a French word, so dropped
# question Ran: counts are mostly "ran", a clipping of heran/heran-; merged to heran, but it is unclear whether "ran" has its own colloquial entry.
# question Sanssouci: the name is French but it is a famous German landmark. The rules cover landmarks with German names, so I kept it.
# question Schär: possibly a cut-off form of Schäre (plural schären), but the lower-case "schär" counts look mostly like the surname/brand; dropped rather than merged.
# question Scirocco: the counts are mostly the VW car; I decided by the lemma's own reading, the wind. Drop is the alternative if you treat it as a brand.
# question Ser: counts are seren/ser/sern; the host word is unclear (possibly a Spanish verb or a fragment), so I dropped it
# question Shorty: Examples show a product/size name and a nickname; I chose drop, but the word could be an established loan.
# question Stanze: all counts are "stanzen" (verb form), but the lemma Stanze is a valid noun, so keep.
# question Streikende: keep or merge:streiken? Treated as a lexicalised participle noun.
# question Stromer: counts may be largely the Swiss e-bike brand; I decided by the lemma's own reading and kept it.
# question SuB: book-community jargon, and its counts are mostly the word "sub", so I dropped it
# question Südafrikaner: I assumed Südafrika is on the learnable place list; if not, this should be dropped.
# question Thais: Counts look like the name or brand "Thais" (steroid forum) and the plural of Thai; I chose merge:Thai, but drop is possible if Thailand is not on the learnable list.
# question Unterstützende: unterstützend may count as an adjective with its own entry; I chose merge:unterstützen under the participle rule
# question Westberlin: a historical city-part name, not on the list, so dropped; merging into Berlin might be intended
# question Westschweiz: a common regional term, but the rules say any place name not on the list is dropped.
# question Württembergische: a regional adjective (mostly in institution names) not on the learnable list; dropped under the place-adjective ruling, but merge:württembergisch is possible.
# question abgedunkelt: it may be an adjective in its own right (like bekannt), but I treated it as a participle of abdunkeln; same for gekreuzt, unterschätzt and verzögert
# question allerbeste: I could not tell whether the dictionary form is allerbest or best. I used allerbest.
# question bluetooth: it is a brand name, but genericised and used as a common noun, so I merged it into Bluetooth.
# question bo: the written forms are mostly "bo" with a few "boden"; Boden is assumed to be the host.
# question brok: the counts are mostly broker, with some broken; I merged to Broker as an established loan, but the lemma's own reading is unclear.
# question button: the lemma is lower-case and the screener flagged "English word"; I treated it as an established loan and merged it to Button, but it could be dropped as English.
# question coloriert: kolorieren vs. colorieren spelling; I chose the Duden main spelling kolorieren
# question ders: "ders" could be der + es or a mis-split of something else; I merged it to der as the host word, though the text does not make clear which word it contracts.
# question einnahmen: no examples and tagged VERB; I read it as the plural-only noun Einnahmen rather than a form of einnehmen.
# question emeritieren: nearly all the counts are the adjective emeritiert; I kept the verb infinitive as the lemma.
# question farb: fragment could be Farbe (noun) or färben/farben; the plural "farben" dominates, so I chose Farbe
# question fernseh: the counts are mostly Fernseher, but the lemma itself is a verb stem; merged to fernsehen, though merge:Fernseher is arguable.
# question fin: the forms (fine, fin, fines) are mostly English/French; no German target, so dropped.
# question fortdauern: nearly all the counts are the participle fortdauernd; I kept the lemma as the verb infinitive. Counts split across forms are otherwise left to the lemmatiser (ruling 13).
# question geleakt: could be kept as an adjective with its own entry, but I merged it into leaken
# question gew: no obvious host word, so I dropped it instead of merging.
# question girocard: I treated it as a genericised product noun and merged it to Girocard, but it could be argued to be a brand and dropped
# question haftend: no dictionary entry as an adjective is certain, so merged into haften
# question harzen: counts are mostly the noun plural Harze, but I kept the lemma by its own reading as the verb harzen
# question hergerissen: tagged VERB, but kept as an adjective with its own entry (hin- und hergerissen); the lemmatiser may want it re-tagged.
# question hingekommen: counts are almost all hinzugekommen (hinzukommen); decided by the lemma's own reading.
# question ibis: counts are mostly the hotel brand, but the lemma could also be the bird (Ibis); dropped under rule 13.
# question ital: could stand for Italien, italienisch or another word, so no merge target is obvious.
# question len: counts (len/lens/lende) are a fragment with no identifiable host word, so I dropped it rather than merging.
# question letztemal: the correct form is the two-word "letztes Mal", so I merged it to Mal.
# question miete: it could be the noun Miete in lower case; I followed the VERB tag and chose mieten.
# question nichtMehr: "nicht mehr" is two words with no single lemma to merge into, so I dropped it.
# question rad: the written forms include gerad and rader, so the counts may belong to another word; decided by the lemma's own reading (Rad).
# question saft: the adjective saft/saftes is rare; I read the lemma as the noun Saft.
# question sonde: counts are mostly "sonder" (another word); decided by the lemma's own reading as the noun Sonde.
# question tools: tagged ADJ and written only as "tools". I treated it as the plural of the established loan Tool, but the rules don't say whether English plurals of loans should merge or drop.
# question tränen: Written forms are mostly verb forms (tränt, tränte, getränt), but the lemma could also be read as the plural of Träne; I kept it as the verb.
# question voten: I treated it as an established loan verb; the ADJ tag is wrong.
# question wartend: I merged it to warten, but "wartende" could be argued as a lexicalised noun or adjective.
# question weitern: the forms weiterte and weitert suggest a verb, but I took the most likely reading as weiter
# question wrden: a conjugated form of werden (würden) with broken encoding; I merged to the dictionary form werden rather than würden.
# question zus: could be zusätzlich or zusammen, so I dropped it rather than merging
# question Wenige: is the headword for wenige/wenigen the lemma wenig, or is wenige its own pronoun or determiner lemma, like einem or einer under ruling 10?
# question ABGB: Austrian civil code, not an institution or ordinary acronym; dropped as a specialist acronym.
# question AHS: Austrian school abbreviation used as word per ruling 12, but counts only tagged as name
# question Antillen: a region with common-noun-like usage (44%); I dropped it as not on the reviewed list, but it could count as a major region.
# question Arab: it could be merged into Araber if the English "Arabs" counts are really German, but the examples are missing, so I dropped it
# question Bielefelder: Bielefeld is a major city, but the rules do not say whether it is on the learnable places list, so its demonym is unresolved.
# question Boho: borderline fashion loan; dropped as not established
# question Boule: the tagger says PROPN, but the examples are ambiguous between the game and a brand name
# question Chilli: the rules do not say whether accepted variant spellings (Chilli vs Chili) merge; merged to the standard form.
# question Coding: borderline loan; dropped as less established than Computer or Software
# question Commitment: borderline English loan; kept because the examples show German business use
# question DOS: the old operating system, kept out as a technical acronym or product name; it is not a clear case.
# question Demokratische Republik Kongo: a country, but not on the learnable list in the batch; dropped as a place name still present
# question Duplex: borderline technical term, kept as a common noun
# question Formulardat: the singular Formulardatum is rare, so I merged to the plural Formulardaten instead of a singular
# question Garni: only the hotel-term reading is kept; no examples were available
# question Grün-Weiß: the examples are club names, but the form is also the ordinary colour adjective
# question Guantanamo: not on the learnable place list, which says to drop remaining places, but it is a very widely known name in German; I kept it, though the strict rule would be drop.
# question HAK: Austrian school type; I treated it as an abbreviation used as a word (ruling 12), though it could also be read as a minor organisation name.
# question Hauer: mostly a surname in the counts, but the common noun (wine grower, tusk) exists; kept by its own reading under ruling 13.
# question Kommunikationsdat: the singular Kommunikationsdatum is rare and Kommunikationsdaten is effectively plural-only, so I merged to the plural.
# question Konjunkturdat: Konjunkturdaten is plural-only in practice, so I merged into it rather than into the singular Konjunkturdatum.
# question Kraftfahrt-Bundesamt: treated as a nationally famous institution; a ministry-like authority could instead be read as dropped under ruling 3.
# question LRS: abbreviation used as a word vs. specialist acronym, kept under ruling 12
# question MPG: it may mean Max-Planck-Gesellschaft (famous institution, keep) or Medizinproduktegesetz (technical); the examples mix both, so I dropped it
# question NEOS: a nationally relevant Austrian party but not named in the rulings; kept as a parliamentary party.
# question OpenSource: written as one word; kept as an established loan (Open Source), but the lemmatiser may prefer a spaced or hyphenated form.
# question PLAYMOBIL: could count as a genericised brand like LEGO; I treated it as an ordinary brand and dropped it.
# question Rabat: capital of Morocco, a well-known city, but it is still in the batch so it is not on the learnable list; dropped.
# question Schwarz-wissen: the target could be capitalised Schwarz-Weiß or lower-case schwarz-weiß (adjective); I chose the noun form
# question Skispring: Skispringen is both a productive infinitive (rule 9: merge to the verb skispringen) and a lexicalised sport noun; I merged to the noun Skispringen.
# question SmartHome: the single-word spelling is a brand-like variant of "Smart Home"; kept as an established loan.
# question Toscana: Toskana is assumed to be on the learnable list; if it is not, this lemma should be dropped as a place name.
# question Venetie: it is a clipped form of Venetien, a region not on the reviewed list, so merging would only point at another name that is dropped
# question Venezia: could be a variant of Venedig, but it only appears in Italian titles, so I dropped it.
# question Vest: forms veste/vesten suggest Veste (fortress) or the Vest region; dropped as a name, but a common noun may be hidden in the counts
# question Zentralamerika: a major region, but it is not on the developer's list and the rules say to drop any place name still in the batch; it may belong on the list
# question Zuschauerinn: merged to the feminine singular Zuschauerin; the Binnen-I ruling might point to Zuschauer instead.
# question allergrößt: kept as its own adjective, but it could be argued to merge into groß
# question ingam: the target "ingame" is a gaming anglicism whose status is borderline, so I merged it instead of dropping
# question jenig: the dictionary lemma could be derjenige or jenige; I chose jenige as the closest target, but derjenige may be preferred.
# question malign: the dictionary form is malign (Duden also gives maligne), but the flag says the bare spelling is never written; kept as the dictionary lemma
# question pubertierend: participle of pubertieren; kept as an adjective with its own use, though it could be merged into the verb
# question safe: German also has the noun Safe (Tresor), but this lemma is tagged ADV and comes from English text, so I dropped it
# question spring: the counts are mixed with Springer, I decided by the lemma's own reading as springen
# question western: could be mostly brand uses (Western Union); kept for the film genre
# question BaWü: should colloquial short forms of listed places be dropped as abbreviations or merged into the place (Baden-Württemberg)?
# question LOS: there are no examples; are the counts los, the noun Los, or fragments of "Los Angeles"?
# question Toscana, Sardini, Zentralamerika: are Toskana, Sardinien and supranational regions such as Zentralamerika on the learnable list? If Toskana or Sardinien is not, change that merge to drop.
# question Agrar: combining form (Agrar-) with no single obvious host word, so dropped rather than merged.
# question Alk: Could be read as a clipping of Alkohol (merge), but "alken" suggests the bird (Alk), so I kept it.
# question Collegium: mostly in proper names; kept as a Duden-listed loan
# question Folkwang: it could count as a famous institution (university and museum in Essen), but I judged it too local to keep.
# question Getötete: I treated it as lexicalised like Angestellte, but it could also count as a productive nominalisation of getötet.
# question Grammy: award name, treated as famous institution, kept
# question Kappa: the examples are all the brand (drop under ruling 5), but the lemma's own reading is the Greek letter; I kept it.
# question Nhe: could be Nähe or nahe/nehme; chose Nähe as the most likely
# question Optimizer: could be treated as an established loan or merged into Optimierer; dropped as a software/product term.
# question POIs: merging to POI would leave a specialist acronym on the list, so I dropped it
# question Pamper: counts are mostly "Pampers" (genericised brand), but the lemma Pamper itself is an English fragment; dropped.
# question Patter: Pattern is an IT loan that may not be on the list; merged to Pattern
# question Reimen: the counts mix the plural noun with the nominalised verb reimen. I chose the noun Reim.
# question Salvia: Most uses are Latin botanical names, but the word is also an established garden-plant name in German, so I kept it.
# question Spik: counts are mostly "Spikes" (sports shoes) and also a character name; merged into Spike.
# question Strassstein: Strass is correctly spelled with ss, so I treated it as not a Swiss variant and kept it.
# question Synchro: could be Synchronisation, Synchronsprecher or Synchronschwimmen; I chose Synchronisation.
# question Ungeimpft: could be a lexicalised nominalisation like Angestellte (keep), but the lemma is capitalised adjective forms, so merged
# question ausgemacht: all written forms are inflected attributive uses, so I kept it as an adjective with its own entry rather than merging to ausmachen.
# question bl: fragment with no obvious target word, dropped
# question dis: could be dialect/garbled dies(e) or the English "dis"; merged to dies as the most likely reading
# question drittgrößte: The ordinal -te rule does not clearly cover compound superlatives, so I kept it.
# question han: counts are mostly "hand", but rule 13 says to decide by the lemma's own reading
# question inter: forms (interes, interen) suggest a cut-off of interessant/interessiert; the target is not certain.
# question iphon: I dropped it as a brand; if iPhone counts as a genericised brand, the right decision would be merge:iPhone.
# question klei: the cut-off fragment could come from kleiden, Kleid or Kleie; I merged into kleiden because it has most of the counts.
# question lan: could be the abbreviation LAN, but counts are mostly land, so treated as a fragment of Land
# question lichtgeschützt: kept as an adjective in its own right; no dictionary verb to merge into
# question nachfahren: counts are mostly Nachfahre (noun), but the lemma reads as the verb nachfahren, so kept per ruling 13
# question sog: no examples; could be Sog (noun), a form of saugen or the abbreviation sog.; chose sogenannt
# question transen: it could be read as the plural of Transe, but the examples are slang or offensive, so I dropped it.
# question verbrauch: forms mix noun, verb and agent noun (verbraucher); merged to the noun
# question wasserlassen: could count as a lexicalised nominalisation (keep as Wasserlassen) instead of a merge to the verb; I treated it as a case fix only
# question sog, klei: when there are no examples and several readings are plausible (sog. vs Sog vs saugen; dialect klei vs Klei), is a best guess acceptable or should these be dropped?
